Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Joe has been hurt on the Internet. Now he is storming around the house, destroying everything in his path.

Joe's house has n floors, each floor is a segment of m cells. Each cell either contains nothing (it is an empty cell), or has a brick or a concrete wall (always something one of three). It is believed that each floor is surrounded by a concrete wall on the left and on the right.

Now Joe is on the n-th floor and in the first cell, counting from left to right. At each moment of time, Joe has the direction of his gaze, to the right or to the left (always one direction of the two). Initially, Joe looks to the right.

Joe moves by a particular algorithm. Every second he makes one of the following actions:   If the cell directly under Joe is empty, then Joe falls down. That is, he moves to this cell, the gaze direction is preserved.  Otherwise consider the next cell in the current direction of the gaze.   If the cell is empty, then Joe moves into it, the gaze direction is preserved.  If this cell has bricks, then Joe breaks them with his forehead (the cell becomes empty), and changes the direction of his gaze to the opposite.  If this cell has a concrete wall, then Joe just changes the direction of his gaze to the opposite (concrete can withstand any number of forehead hits).  

Joe calms down as soon as he reaches any cell of the first floor.

The figure below shows an example Joe's movements around the house.

 [Image] 

Determine how many seconds Joe will need to calm down.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m (2 ≤ n ≤ 100, 1 ≤ m ≤ 10^4).

Next n lines contain the description of Joe's house. The i-th of these lines contains the description of the (n - i + 1)-th floor of the house — a line that consists of m characters: "." means an empty cell, "+" means bricks and "#" means a concrete wall.

It is guaranteed that the first cell of the n-th floor is empty.


-----Output-----

Print a single number — the number of seconds Joe needs to reach the first floor; or else, print word "Never" (without the quotes), if it can never happen.

Please, do not use the %lld specifier to read or write 64-bit integers in С++. It is preferred to use the cin, cout streams or the %I64d specifier.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 5
..+.#
#+..+
+.#+.

Output
14
Input
4 10
...+.##+.+
+#++..+++#
++.#++++..
.+##.++#.+

Output
42

Input
2 2
..
++

Output
Never

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout


Now solve the problem and return the code.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. Joe starts on the n-th floor (bottom floor) in the first cell, looking right
2. Each second, Joe performs one of these actions:
   - If the cell directly below him is empty, he falls down
   - Otherwise, he looks in his current direction and:
     - If the next cell is empty, he moves there (direction preserved)
     - If the next cell has bricks, he breaks them (cell becomes empty) and reverses direction
     - If the next cell has concrete wall, he reverses direction
3. Joe calms down when he reaches any cell on the first floor (top floor)
4. We need to find how many seconds it takes, or "Never" if it never happens

## Key Observations

1. Joe can only fall down to empty cells below him
2. When he hits bricks, he breaks them and changes direction
3. When he hits concrete walls, he only changes direction
4. The process might loop indefinitely if Joe keeps bouncing between cells

## Algorithm Approach

1. Use BFS or simulation with state tracking
2. State = (current_floor, current_position, current_direction)
3. For each state, simulate one second
4. Track visited states to detect cycles (infinite loop)
5. If we reach floor 1, return the time
6. If we detect a cycle without reaching floor 1, return "Never"

## Step-by-step Solution

```python
from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read the floors (n lines, where i-th line is (n-i+1)-th floor)
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # Convert floors to 0-indexed: floor 0 is top (first floor), floor n-1 is bottom (n-th floor)
    # So we have floors[0] = first floor, floors[n-1] = nth floor
    
    # Joe starts at floor n-1 (bottom), position 0, looking right (1 for right, 0 for left)
    start_floor = n - 1
    start_pos = 0
    start_dir = 1  # 1 = right, 0 = left
    
    # Directions: 1 = right, 0 = left
    # State: (floor, pos, dir)
    visited = set()
    queue = deque()
    
    # Initialize queue with starting state and time 0
    queue.append((start_floor, start_pos, start_dir, 0))
    visited.add((start_floor, start_pos, start_dir))
    
    while queue:
        floor, pos, dir, time = queue.popleft()
        
        # Check if we reached the first floor (top floor, index 0)
        if floor == 0:
            return time
        
        # Get current cell content
        cell_content = floors[floor]
        
        # If directly below is empty, Joe falls down
        if floor < n - 1:  # There's a floor below
            below_pos = pos
            below_content = floors[floor + 1][below_pos]
            
            if below_content == '.':
                # Joe falls down to the cell below
                new_floor = floor + 1
                new_pos = pos
                new_dir = dir  # direction preserved
                
                if (new_floor, new_pos, new_dir) not in visited:
                    visited.add((new_floor, new_pos, new_dir))
                    queue.append((new_floor, new_pos, new_dir, time + 1))
        
        # Otherwise, Joe moves in current direction
        next_pos = pos
        if dir == 1:  # right
            next_pos = pos + 1
        else:  # left
            next_pos = pos - 1
        
        # Check if next_pos is valid
        if next_pos < 0 or next_pos >= m:
            # If out of bounds, simply reverse direction
            new_dir = 1 - dir
            new_floor = floor
            new_pos = pos
            
            if (new_floor, new_pos, new_dir) not in visited:
                visited.add((new_floor, new_pos, new_dir))
                queue.append((new_floor, new_pos, new_dir, time + 1))
        else:
            next_content = floors[floor][next_pos]
            
            if next_content == '.':
                # Empty cell - move there
                new_floor = floor
                new_pos = next_pos
                new_dir = dir
                
                if (new_floor, new_pos, new_dir) not in visited:
                    visited.add((new_floor, new_pos, new_dir))
                    queue.append((new_floor, new_pos, new_dir, time + 1))
            elif next_content == '+':
                # Brick - break it and reverse direction
                new_floor = floor
                new_pos = next_pos
                new_dir = 1 - dir
                
                # Update the floor - the brick is now empty
                # We don't actually change the board state in simulation
                # But we need to account for the fact that the cell becomes empty
                
                # Check if this state has been visited
                if (new_floor, new_pos, new_dir) not in visited:
                    visited.add((new_floor, new_pos, new_dir))
                    queue.append((new_floor, new_pos, new_dir, time + 1))
            else:  # next_content == '#'
                # Concrete wall - reverse direction
                new_floor = floor
                new_pos = pos
                new_dir = 1 - dir
                
                if (new_floor, new_pos, new_dir) not in visited:
                    visited.add((new_floor, new_pos, new_dir))
                    queue.append((new_floor, new_pos, new_dir, time + 1))
    
    return "Never"

print(solve())
```

Wait, I need to reconsider the problem. The issue is that when Joe breaks a brick, the cell becomes empty, which affects future paths. Also, in my simulation, I'm not properly handling the falling down logic.

Let me fix the solution:

```python
from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read the floors
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # State: (floor, pos, dir)
    # We'll use BFS to explore all possible states
    visited = set()
    queue = deque()
    
    # Start at bottom floor (n-1), position 0, direction right (1)
    start_state = (n - 1, 0, 1)
    queue.append((start_state, 0))
    visited.add(start_state)
    
    # The house is stored such that floors[i] corresponds to floor (n-1-i)
    # So floors[0] is top floor, floors[n-1] is bottom floor
    
    while queue:
        (floor, pos, dir), time = queue.popleft()
        
        # Check if we reached first floor (top floor)
        if floor == 0:
            return time
        
        # Check if we can fall down
        if floor < n - 1:  # There's a floor below
            below_pos = pos
            below_cell = floors[floor + 1][below_pos]
            
            if below_cell == '.':
                # Fall down to the cell below
                new_state = (floor + 1, pos, dir)
                if new_state not in visited:
                    visited.add(new_state)
                    queue.append((new_state, time + 1))
        
        # Move in current direction
        next_pos = pos
        if dir == 1:  # right
            next_pos = pos + 1
        else:  # left
            next_pos = pos - 1
        
        # Check if next position is valid
        if next_pos >= 0 and next_pos < m:
            next_cell = floors[floor][next_pos]
            
            if next_cell == '.':
                # Empty cell - move there
                new_state = (floor, next_pos, dir)
                if new_state not in visited:
                    visited.add(new_state)
                    queue.append((new_state, time + 1))
            elif next_cell == '+':
                # Brick - break it and change direction
                new_state = (floor, next_pos, 1 - dir)
                # The brick becomes empty, so future movements will have this cell as empty
                if new_state not in visited:
                    visited.add(new_state)
                    queue.append((new_state, time + 1))
            else:  # next_cell == '#'
                # Concrete wall - change direction only
                new_state = (floor, pos, 1 - dir)
                if new_state not in visited:
                    visited.add(new_state)
                    queue.append((new_state, time + 1))
        else:
            # Out of bounds - change direction
            new_state = (floor, pos, 1 - dir)
            if new_state not in visited:
                visited.add(new_state)
                queue.append((new_state, time + 1))
    
    return "Never"

print(solve())
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. Joe starts on the n-th floor (bottom floor) at position 1 (leftmost cell) looking right
2. Each second, Joe makes one of these actions:
   - If the cell directly below is empty, Joe falls down
   - Otherwise, he looks in his current direction and:
     - If the cell is empty, he moves there (direction unchanged)
     - If the cell has bricks (+), he breaks them (becomes empty), changes direction
     - If the cell has concrete wall (#), he changes direction only
3. Joe stops when he reaches any cell on the first floor
4. We need to find how many seconds it takes, or "Never" if impossible

## Key Observations

1. Joe falls down when the cell below him is empty - this is a major movement
2. When Joe encounters bricks or walls, he changes direction
3. The game ends when Joe reaches floor 1
4. We need to simulate Joe's movement second by second

## Algorithm

1. Parse input - store the grid
2. Simulate Joe's movement second by second:
   - Start at position (n, 0) on floor n (0-indexed), direction = right (1)
   - For each second:
     - Check if cell below is empty: if yes, fall down
     - Otherwise, check next cell in current direction:
       - If empty: move there
       - If brick (+): break it, change direction
       - If wall (#): change direction only
3. When Joe reaches floor 1 (index 0), stop and return seconds
4. If we enter a cycle without reaching floor 1, return "Never"

## Important Considerations

- We need to detect cycles to avoid infinite loops
- The state consists of (floor, position, direction)
- If we visit the same state twice, we're in a cycle and will never reach floor 1

## Implementation

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read the floors (n rows, each with m characters)
    # floors[i] represents floor (n-i) - so floors[0] is the top floor
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # Joe starts at floor n-1 (0-indexed), position 0, looking right (direction = 1)
    floor = n - 1
    pos = 0
    direction = 1  # 1 for right, -1 for left
    
    # Track visited states to detect cycles
    visited = set()
    
    seconds = 0
    max_seconds = n * m * 2  # reasonable upper bound to prevent infinite loop
    
    while floor > 0:  # while not on first floor
        # Check if we've seen this state before
        state = (floor, pos, direction)
        if state in visited:
            print("Never")
            return
        
        visited.add(state)
        
        seconds += 1
        
        # Check if cell directly below is empty
        if floor > 0 and floors[floor - 1][pos] == '.':
            # Joe falls down to the next floor
            floor -= 1
            continue
        
        # Otherwise, move in current direction
        next_pos = pos
        if direction == 1:  # going right
            next_pos = pos + 1
        else:  # going left
            next_pos = pos - 1
        
        # Check bounds
        if next_pos < 0 or next_pos >= m:
            # This shouldn't happen with the wall boundaries, but just in case
            # If we hit wall, change direction
            direction *= -1
            continue
        
        cell = floors[floor][next_pos]
        
        if cell == '.':
            # Empty cell - move there
            pos = next_pos
        elif cell == '+':
            # Brick - break it, change direction
            floors[floor] = floors[floor][:next_pos] + '.' + floors[floor][next_pos + 1:]
            direction *= -1
        elif cell == '#':
            # Concrete wall - change direction only
            direction *= -1
    
    print(seconds)

solve()
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. Joe starts on the n-th floor (bottom floor) at the first cell (leftmost)
2. He initially looks to the right
3. Each second, he follows these rules:
   - If the cell directly below him is empty, he falls down (moves to that cell, direction unchanged)
   - Otherwise, he looks in his current direction and:
     - If the next cell in that direction is empty, he moves there (direction unchanged)
     - If the next cell has bricks, he breaks them (becomes empty), changes direction to opposite
     - If the next cell has concrete wall, he changes direction to opposite
4. Joe stops when he reaches any cell on the first floor (top floor)
5. We need to count the seconds (steps) until he reaches the first floor, or output "Never" if he never does

## Key Observations

1. The floor description is given in reverse order: the i-th line describes the (n-i+1)-th floor
2. Joe moves cell by cell, and we need to simulate his movement
3. We need to track:
   - Current floor and position
   - Current direction (left or right)
   - The state of each cell (empty, brick, concrete)
4. Since concrete walls are indestructible and Joe just changes direction when hitting them, we need to detect cycles

## Algorithm

1. Parse input and store the floors
2. Initialize position on n-th floor (0-indexed), at position 0, direction = "right"
3. Use simulation with a visited state to detect cycles (if we visit the same (floor, position, direction) state, we're in a loop)
4. In each step:
   - Check if we can fall down (cell below is empty)
   - Otherwise, check the next cell in current direction
   - Apply the movement rules
5. Stop when we reach floor 0 (first floor) or detect a cycle

## Implementation

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read floors - the i-th line describes floor (n-i+1)
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # Convert floors to list of lists for easier manipulation
    # floors[i] represents the (n-i)-th floor (0-indexed from top)
    # So floors[0] is the 1st floor (top), floors[n-1] is the nth floor (bottom)
    # But the input gives us from bottom to top, so we need to reverse
    # Actually, the problem says: "the i-th of these lines contains the description of the (n-i+1)-th floor"
    # So the first line is floor n, second line is floor n-1, etc.
    # So floors[i] is floor (n-i) from bottom to top
    
    # Reorganize: floors[i] = floor (n-i-1) from bottom
    # We want floor 0 (top) to be at index n-1
    house = [list(line) for line in floors]
    
    # Current position: (floor, pos)
    # Start at bottom floor (n-1), position 0
    floor = n - 1
    pos = 0
    direction = "right"  # "right" or "left"
    
    # To detect cycles, we store visited states: (floor, pos, direction)
    visited = set()
    seconds = 0
    
    while floor > 0:  # While not on first floor (top floor)
        state = (floor, pos, direction)
        if state in visited:
            print("Never")
            return
            
        visited.add(state)
        
        seconds += 1
        
        # Check if we can fall down
        if floor > 0 and house[floor - 1][pos] == '.':
            # Fall down to the floor below
            floor -= 1
            # Direction remains the same
            continue
        
        # Otherwise, look in current direction
        next_pos = pos
        if direction == "right":
            next_pos = pos + 1
        else:  # direction == "left"
            next_pos = pos - 1
        
        # Check if next position is within bounds
        if next_pos < 0 or next_pos >= m:
            # This shouldn't happen because of concrete walls
            # But if it does, we change direction
            direction = "left" if direction == "right" else "right"
            continue
        
        # Check the cell at next_pos
        if house[floor][next_pos] == '.':
            # Move to empty cell
            pos = next_pos
        elif house[floor][next_pos] == '+':
            # Brick - break it and change direction
            house[floor][next_pos] = '.'
            direction = "left" if direction == "right" else "right"
        elif house[floor][next_pos] == '#':
            # Concrete wall - change direction only
            direction = "left" if direction == "right" else "right"
    
    # We reached the first floor (top floor)
    print(seconds)

solve()
```

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Joe has been hurt on the Internet. Now he is storming around the house, destroying everything in his path.

Joe's house has n floors, each floor is a segment of m cells. Each cell either contains nothing (it is an empty cell), or has a brick or a concrete wall (always something one of three). It is believed that each floor is surrounded by a concrete wall on the left and on the right.

Now Joe is on the n-th floor and in the first cell, counting from left to right. At each moment of time, Joe has the direction of his gaze, to the right or to the left (always one direction of the two). Initially, Joe looks to the right.

Joe moves by a particular algorithm. Every second he makes one of the following actions:   If the cell directly under Joe is empty, then Joe falls down. That is, he moves to this cell, the gaze direction is preserved.  Otherwise consider the next cell in the current direction of the gaze.   If the cell is empty, then Joe moves into it, the gaze direction is preserved.  If this cell has bricks, then Joe breaks them with his forehead (the cell becomes empty), and changes the direction of his gaze to the opposite.  If this cell has a concrete wall, then Joe just changes the direction of his gaze to the opposite (concrete can withstand any number of forehead hits).  

Joe calms down as soon as he reaches any cell of the first floor.

The figure below shows an example Joe's movements around the house.

 [Image] 

Determine how many seconds Joe will need to calm down.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m (2 ≤ n ≤ 100, 1 ≤ m ≤ 10^4).

Next n lines contain the description of Joe's house. The i-th of these lines contains the description of the (n - i + 1)-th floor of the house — a line that consists of m characters: "." means an empty cell, "+" means bricks and "#" means a concrete wall.

It is guaranteed that the first cell of the n-th floor is empty.


-----Output-----

Print a single number — the number of seconds Joe needs to reach the first floor; or else, print word "Never" (without the quotes), if it can never happen.

Please, do not use the %lld specifier to read or write 64-bit integers in С++. It is preferred to use the cin, cout streams or the %I64d specifier.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 5
..+.#
#+..+
+.#+.

Output
14
Input
4 10
...+.##+.+
+#++..+++#
++.#++++..
.+##.++#.+

Output
42

Input
2 2
..
++

Output
Never

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. Joe starts on the n-th floor (bottom floor) at position 0 (first cell) looking to the right
2. Each second, Joe performs one action:
   - If the cell directly below him is empty, he falls down
   - Otherwise, he looks in his current direction and:
     - If the cell in that direction is empty, he moves there
     - If the cell has bricks (+), he breaks them (becomes empty), flips his direction
     - If the cell has concrete wall (#), he flips his direction (no damage)
3. Joe stops when he reaches any cell on the first floor
4. We need to find how many seconds it takes, or "Never" if impossible

## Key Insights

1. This is a state-based simulation problem where the state is (floor, position, direction)
2. We need to avoid infinite loops - if we visit the same state twice, we're in an infinite loop
3. The state space is manageable since n ≤ 100 and m ≤ 10^4
4. We need to track which states we've seen to detect cycles

## Algorithm Design

1. Use BFS or simulation with a visited set to detect cycles
2. For each state (floor, position, direction), simulate one second of movement
3. Track the number of seconds passed
4. If we reach floor 0, return the time
5. If we detect a repeated state, return "Never"

## Step-by-Step Solution

Let me trace through the first example:
- n=3, m=5
- Floor 1: "..+.#" (bottom)
- Floor 2: "#+..+"
- Floor 3: "+.#+." (top)

Joe starts at floor 3, position 0, direction right.

I'll implement the simulation with state tracking:

```python
from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read the floors (reverse order since input gives n-th floor first)
    floors = []
    for i in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # Convert to list of lists for easier manipulation
    # floors[i] is the (n-i)-th floor (0-indexed from bottom)
    # So floors[0] is the bottom floor (n-th floor)
    # floors[n-1] is the top floor (1-st floor)
    
    # State: (floor_index, position, direction)
    # direction: 0 for left, 1 for right
    # We use a set to track visited states to detect cycles
    
    visited = set()
    queue = deque()
    
    # Initial state: start at floor 0 (bottom), position 0, direction 1 (right)
    start_state = (0, 0, 1)  # (floor, position, direction)
    queue.append((0, 0, 1, 0))  # (floor, position, direction, time)
    visited.add((0, 0, 1))
    
    while queue:
        floor, pos, dir_, time = queue.popleft()
        
        # Check if we reached the first floor (top floor, index n-1)
        if floor == n - 1:
            print(time)
            return
            
        # Try to move
        next_floor, next_pos, next_dir = floor, pos, dir_
        
        # Check if we can fall down (cell directly below is empty)
        below_pos = pos
        if below_pos < m and floor + 1 < n and floors[floor + 1][below_pos] == '.':
            # Fall down to next floor at same position
            next_floor = floor + 1
            next_pos = pos
            next_dir = dir_  # direction preserved
        else:
            # Look in current direction
            if dir_ == 1:  # looking right
                next_pos = pos + 1
                if next_pos >= m:
                    # Hit concrete wall on right
                    next_dir = 0  # flip to left
                    next_pos = pos  # stay in same position
                else:
                    cell = floors[floor][next_pos]
                    if cell == '.':
                        # Move to empty cell
                        next_pos = next_pos
                        next_dir = dir_
                    elif cell == '+':
                        # Break brick, flip direction
                        # Convert brick to empty
                        new_floor_state = list(floors[floor])
                        new_floor_state[next_pos] = '.'
                        floors[floor] = ''.join(new_floor_state)
                        next_dir = 1 - dir_  # flip direction
                    elif cell == '#':
                        # Hit concrete wall, flip direction
                        next_dir = 1 - dir_
            else:  # looking left
                next_pos = pos - 1
                if next_pos < 0:
                    # Hit concrete wall on left
                    next_dir = 1  # flip to right
                    next_pos = pos  # stay in same position
                else:
                    cell = floors[floor][next_pos]
                    if cell == '.':
                        # Move to empty cell
                        next_pos = next_pos
                        next_dir = dir_
                    elif cell == '+':
                        # Break brick, flip direction
                        new_floor_state = list(floors[floor])
                        new_floor_state[next_pos] = '.'
                        floors[floor] = ''.join(new_floor_state)
                        next_dir = 1 - dir_  # flip direction
                    elif cell == '#':
                        # Hit concrete wall, flip direction
                        next_dir = 1 - dir_
        
        # Create new state
        new_state = (next_floor, next_pos, next_dir)
        
        # Check if we've seen this state before - if yes, infinite loop
        if new_state in visited:
            continue
            
        # Add to queue if not visited
        visited.add(new_state)
        queue.append((next_floor, next_pos, next_dir, time + 1))
    
    # If we exit the loop without finding a solution, it's never
    print("Never")

solve()
```

Wait, I see an issue in my logic. I'm modifying the floors array during simulation, which might not be appropriate for state tracking. Let me reconsider.

Actually, I need to properly track the state without permanently modifying the input. Let me fix this:

```python
from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read the floors
    floors = []
    for i in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # State: (floor, position, direction)
    # We need to track the actual state of the floors to determine what happens
    # But we can't modify the original input, so we need to simulate state transitions
    
    # Use visited set to detect cycles
    visited = set()
    
    # Initial state: start at floor 0, position 0, direction 1 (right)
    queue = deque()
    queue.append((0, 0, 1, 0))  # (floor, position, direction, time)
    visited.add((0, 0, 1))
    
    # Create a function to get the next state
    def get_next_state(floor, pos, dir_):
        # Check if we can fall down
        if floor + 1 < n and floors[floor + 1][pos] == '.':
            return (floor + 1, pos, dir_)
        
        # Otherwise look in current direction
        if dir_ == 1:  # right
            next_pos = pos + 1
            if next_pos >= m:
                # Hit wall - flip direction
                return (floor, pos, 0)
            else:
                cell = floors[floor][next_pos]
                if cell == '.':
                    # Move to empty cell
                    return (floor, next_pos, dir_)
                elif cell == '+':
                    # Break brick, flip direction
                    # For simulation, we just flip direction
                    return (floor, next_pos, 1 - dir_)
                elif cell == '#':
                    # Hit concrete wall, flip direction
                    return (floor, next_pos, 1 - dir_)
        else:  # left
            next_pos = pos - 1
            if next_pos < 0:
                # Hit wall - flip direction
                return (floor, pos, 1)
            else:
                cell = floors[floor][next_pos]
                if cell == '.':
                    # Move to empty cell
                    return (floor, next_pos, dir_)
                elif cell == '+':
                    # Break brick, flip direction
                    return (floor, next_pos, 1 - dir_)
                elif cell == '#':
                    # Hit concrete wall, flip direction
                    return (floor, next_pos, 1 - dir_)
        
        return (floor, pos, dir_)  # fallback
    
    while queue:
        floor, pos, dir_, time = queue.popleft()
        
        # Check if we reached the first floor (top floor, index n-1)
        if floor == n - 1:
            print(time)
            return
            
        # Get next state
        next_floor, next_pos, next_dir = get_next_state(floor, pos, dir_)
        
        # Check if we've seen this state before - if yes, infinite loop
        if (next_floor, next_pos, next_dir) in visited:
            continue
            
        # Add to visited and queue
        visited.add((next_floor, next_pos, next_dir))
        queue.append((next_floor, next_pos, next_dir, time + 1))
    
    # If we exit the loop without finding a solution, it's never
    print("Never")

solve()
```

```python
from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read the floors
    floors = []
    for i in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # We need to track the actual state of the floors
    # Since bricks become empty when broken, we need to modify them
    # But we need to track states to detect cycles
    
    # State: (floor, position, direction)
    # We use a set to track visited states
    visited = set()
    
    # Initial state
    queue = deque()
    queue.append((0, 0, 1, 0))  # (floor, pos, dir, time)
    visited.add((0, 0, 1))
    
    # Make a copy of the floors to work with
    current_floors = [list(f) for f in floors]
    
    while queue:
        floor, pos, dir_, time = queue.popleft()
        
        # Check if reached first floor
        if floor == n - 1:
            print(time)
            return
            
        # Determine next action
        next_floor, next_pos, next_dir = floor, pos, dir_
        
        # Check if can fall down
        if floor + 1 < n and current_floors[floor + 1][pos] == '.':
            next_floor = floor + 1
            next_pos = pos
            next_dir = dir_
        else:
            # Look in current direction
            if dir_ == 1:  # right
                next_pos = pos + 1
                if next_pos >= m:
                    # Hit right wall - flip direction
                    next_dir = 0
                else:
                    cell = current_floors[floor][next_pos]
                    if cell == '.':
                        # Move to empty cell
                        next_pos = next_pos
                        next_dir = dir_
                    elif cell == '+':
                        # Break brick - make it empty, flip direction
                        current_floors[floor][next_pos] = '.'
                        next_dir = 1 - dir_
                    elif cell == '#':
                        # Hit concrete wall - flip direction
                        next_dir = 1 - dir_
            else:  # left
                next_pos = pos - 1
                if next_pos < 0:
                    # Hit left wall - flip direction
                    next_dir = 1
                else:
                    cell = current_floors[floor][next_pos]
                    if cell == '.':
                        # Move to empty cell
                        next_pos = next_pos
                        next_dir = dir_
                    elif cell == '+':
                        # Break brick - make it empty, flip direction
                        current_floors[floor][next_pos] = '.'
                        next_dir = 1 - dir_
                    elif cell == '#':
                        # Hit concrete wall - flip direction
                        next_dir = 1 - dir_
        
        # Create new state
        new_state = (next_floor, next_pos, next_dir)
        
        # Check if we've seen this state before - if yes, infinite loop
        if new_state in visited:
            continue
            
        # Add to visited and queue
        visited.add(new_state)
        queue.append((next_floor, next_pos, next_dir, time + 1))
    
    print("Never")

solve()
```

    n, m = map(int, input().split())

    floors = []
    for i in range(n):

            next_pos = pos
            next_dir = dir_

                        next_pos = next_pos
                        next_dir = dir_

                        next_dir = 1 - dir_

                    next_dir = 1

                        # Move to empty cell
                        next_pos = next_pos
                        next_dir = dir_
                    elif cell == '+':
                        # Break brick - make it empty, flip direction
                        current_floors[floor][next_pos] = '.'
                        next_dir = 1 - dir_
                    elif cell == '#':
                        # Hit concrete wall - flip direction
                        next_dir = 1 - dir_
        
        # Create new state
        new_state = (next_floor, next_pos, next_dir)
        
        # Check if we've seen this state before - if yes, infinite loop
        if new_state in visited:
            continue
            
        # Add to visited and queue
        visited.add(new_state)
        queue.append((next_floor, next_pos, next_dir, time + 1))
    
    print("Never")

solve()

Joe's house has n floors, each floor is a segment of m cells. Each cell either contains nothing (it is an empty cell), or has a brick or a concrete wall (always something one of three). It is believed that each floor is surrounded by a concrete wall on the left and on the right.

It is guaranteed that the first cell of the n-th floor is empty.

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. Joe starts on the nth floor (bottom floor) at position 0 (first cell), looking right
2. Each second, Joe performs one of these actions:
   - If the cell directly below is empty, he falls down (moves down, direction unchanged)
   - Otherwise, he moves in his current direction:
     - If the cell is empty, he moves there (direction unchanged)
     - If the cell has bricks ("+"), he breaks them (cell becomes empty), changes direction
     - If the cell has concrete wall ("#"), he changes direction only
3. Joe stops when he reaches any cell on the first floor
4. We need to count the seconds it takes, or output "Never" if it never happens

## Key Insights

1. This is a state-based simulation problem
2. State includes: current floor, current position, current direction
3. We need to detect cycles to avoid infinite loops
4. The state space is finite (n floors × m positions × 2 directions), so cycles will eventually occur
5. If we detect a cycle without reaching the first floor, we return "Never"

## Algorithm Steps

1. Use BFS or DFS with state tracking to simulate Joe's movement
2. State: (floor, position, direction)
3. For each state, calculate the next move according to the rules
4. Track visited states to detect cycles
5. If we reach floor 0, return the number of seconds
6. If we detect a cycle without reaching floor 0, return "Never"

## Implementation

```python
from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read the house layout
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # Joe starts on floor n-1 (0-indexed), position 0, looking right (0 = right, 1 = left)
    start_floor = n - 1
    start_pos = 0
    start_dir = 0  # 0 = right, 1 = left
    
    # Use BFS to simulate the process
    # State: (floor, position, direction, time)
    queue = deque([(start_floor, start_pos, start_dir, 0)])
    
    # Set to track visited states to detect cycles
    visited = set()
    visited.add((start_floor, start_pos, start_dir))
    
    while queue:
        floor, pos, dir_, time = queue.popleft()
        
        # Check if we reached the first floor (floor 0)
        if floor == 0:
            return time
        
        # Calculate next position and direction
        next_floor = floor
        next_pos = pos
        next_dir = dir_
        
        # Check if we can fall down
        if floor < n - 1:  # There's a floor below
            below_pos = pos
            below_cell = floors[floor + 1][below_pos]
            
            if below_cell == '.':
                # Fall down
                next_floor = floor + 1
                next_pos = pos
                next_dir = dir_
            else:
                # Can't fall down, so we move in current direction
                if dir_ == 0:  # Looking right
                    next_pos = pos + 1
                    if next_pos >= m:
                        # We hit a concrete wall on the right
                        next_dir = 1
                        next_pos = pos  # Stay in same position
                    else:
                        next_cell = floors[floor][next_pos]
                        if next_cell == '.':
                            # Empty cell - move there
                            next_pos = next_pos
                            next_dir = dir_
                        elif next_cell == '+':
                            # Brick - break it, change direction
                            next_pos = next_pos
                            next_dir = 1 - dir_
                            floors[floor] = floors[floor][:next_pos] + '.' + floors[floor][next_pos+1:]
                        elif next_cell == '#':
                            # Concrete wall - change direction only
                            next_dir = 1 - dir_
                else:  # Looking left
                    next_pos = pos - 1
                    if next_pos < 0:
                        # We hit a concrete wall on the left
                        next_dir = 0
                        next_pos = pos  # Stay in same position
                    else:
                        next_cell = floors[floor][next_pos]
                        if next_cell == '.':
                            # Empty cell - move there
                            next_pos = next_pos
                            next_dir = dir_
                        elif next_cell == '+':
                            # Brick - break it, change direction
                            next_pos = next_pos
                            next_dir = 1 - dir_
                            floors[floor] = floors[floor][:next_pos] + '.' + floors[floor][next_pos+1:]
                        elif next_cell == '#':
                            # Concrete wall - change direction only
                            next_dir = 1 - dir_
        
        # Check if the new state has been visited
        new_state = (next_floor, next_pos, next_dir)
        if new_state in visited:
            # We have a cycle without reaching floor 0
            continue
            
        visited.add(new_state)
        queue.append((next_floor, next_pos, next_dir, time + 1))
    
    return "Never"

print(solve())
```

```python
from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read the house layout
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(list(input().strip()))
    
    # Joe starts on floor n-1 (0-indexed), position 0, looking right (0 = right, 1 = left)
    start_floor = n - 1
    start_pos = 0
    start_dir = 0  # 0 = right, 1 = left
    
    # Use BFS to simulate the process
    # State: (floor, position, direction)
    queue = deque([(start_floor, start_pos, start_dir)])
    
    # Set to track visited states to detect cycles
    visited = set()
    visited.add((start_floor, start_pos, start_dir))
    
    time = 0
    
    while queue:
        # Process all states at current time level
        next_queue = []
        
        for floor, pos, dir_ in queue:
            time += 1
            
            # Check if we reached the first floor
            if floor == 0:
                return time
            
            # Try to fall down if possible
            if floor < n - 1:  # There's a floor below
                below_cell = floors[floor + 1][pos]
                if below_cell == '.':
                    # Fall down to the next floor at same position
                    next_queue.append((floor + 1, pos, dir_))
                else:
                    # Can't fall down, so we move in current direction
                    if dir_ == 0:  # Looking right
                        next_pos = pos + 1
                        if next_pos >= m:
                            # Hit right wall - change direction
                            next_dir = 1
                            next_pos = pos
                        else:
                            next_cell = floors[floor][next_pos]
                            if next_cell == '.':
                                # Empty cell - move there
                                next_queue.append((floor, next_pos, dir_))
                            elif next_cell == '+':
                                # Brick - break it, change direction
                                floors[floor][next_pos] = '.'
                                next_dir = 1 - dir_
                                next_queue.append((floor, next_pos, next_dir))
                            elif next_cell == '#':
                                # Concrete wall - change direction only
                                next_dir = 1 - dir_
                                next_queue.append((floor, next_pos, next_dir))
                    else:  # Looking left
                        next_pos = pos - 1
                        if next_pos < 0:
                            # Hit left wall - change direction
                            next_dir = 0
                            next_pos = pos
                        else:
                            next_cell = floors[floor][next_pos]
                            if next_cell == '.':
                                # Empty cell - move there
                                next_queue.append((floor, next_pos, dir_))
                            elif next_cell == '+':
                                # Brick - break it, change direction
                                floors[floor][next_pos] = '.'
                                next_dir = 1 - dir_
                                next_queue.append((floor, next_pos, next_dir))
                            elif next_cell == '#':
                                # Concrete wall - change direction only
                                next_dir = 1 - dir_
                                next_queue.append((floor, next_pos, next_dir))
        
        # Add all next states to the queue
        queue = deque(next_queue)
    
    return "Never"
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. Joe starts on the n-th floor (top floor) at position 1 (leftmost cell)
2. He initially looks to the right
3. Each second, he follows these rules:
   - If the cell directly below him is empty, he falls down
   - Otherwise, he looks in his current direction and:
     - If next cell is empty, he moves there (direction stays same)
     - If next cell has bricks, he breaks them (cell becomes empty), changes direction
     - If next cell has concrete wall, he changes direction only (wall withstands hits)
4. He stops when he reaches any cell on the first floor
5. We need to count the total seconds until he calms down

## Key Insights

1. Joe can only fall down when the cell below is empty
2. When he hits bricks, he breaks them and changes direction
3. When he hits concrete walls, he only changes direction
4. We need to simulate his movements second by second
5. We must detect if it will never reach the first floor (infinite loop)

## Algorithm Approach

1. Use simulation with explicit state tracking
2. State: (current_floor, current_position, current_direction)
3. At each step:
   - Check if current floor is floor 1 → return seconds
   - Check if cell below is empty → fall down to next floor
   - Otherwise, check next cell in current direction:
     - If empty → move there
     - If brick → break it, change direction
     - If concrete wall → change direction
4. Use a visited state set to detect cycles (infinite loop)

## Implementation Steps

1. Parse input to get floor layout
2. Initialize state with (n, 0, "right") - 0-indexed position
3. Simulate until reaching floor 1 or detecting a cycle
4. Return time or "Never" if cycle detected

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read floor descriptions
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # Convert to 0-indexed: floor 0 is first floor, floor n-1 is nth floor
    # Joe starts at floor n-1 (0-indexed), position 0, direction "right"
    
    # State: (floor, position, direction)
    # direction: "right" or "left"
    
    # Track visited states to detect cycles
    visited = set()
    
    floor = n - 1  # start on n-th floor (0-indexed)
    pos = 0        # start at first cell (0-indexed)
    direction = "right"
    time = 0
    
    while floor > 0:  # while not on first floor (floor 0)
        # Create state key for cycle detection
        state = (floor, pos, direction)
        if state in visited:
            print("Never")
            return
            
        visited.add(state)
        
        # Check if we can fall down
        below_floor = floor - 1
        below_pos = pos
        
        # If cell directly below is empty, fall down
        if floors[below_floor][below_pos] == '.':
            floor -= 1
            time += 1
            # If we reached first floor, stop
            if floor == 0:
                break
        else:
            # We cannot fall down, need to move in current direction
            next_pos = pos
            if direction == "right":
                next_pos = pos + 1
                if next_pos >= m:
                    # Hit concrete wall on right
                    direction = "left"
                    time += 1
                elif floors[floor][next_pos] == '#':
                    # Hit concrete wall
                    direction = "left"
                    time += 1
                elif floors[floor][next_pos] == '+':
                    # Hit brick, break it and change direction
                    floors[floor] = floors[floor][:next_pos] + '.' + floors[floor][next_pos+1:]
                    direction = "left"
                    time += 1
                else:  # empty cell
                    # Move to empty cell
                    pos = next_pos
                    time += 1
            else:  # direction == "left"
                next_pos = pos - 1
                if next_pos < 0:
                    # Hit concrete wall on left
                    direction = "right"
                    time += 1
                elif floors[floor][next_pos] == '#':
                    # Hit concrete wall
                    direction = "right"
                    time += 1
                elif floors[floor][next_pos] == '+':
                    # Hit brick, break it and change direction
                    floors[floor] = floors[floor][:next_pos] + '.' + floors[floor][next_pos+1:]
                    direction = "right"
                    time += 1
                else:  # empty cell
                    # Move to empty cell
                    pos = next_pos
                    time += 1
    
    print(time)
```

```python
        if floors[below_floor][below_pos] == '.':
            # Fall down
            floor -= 1
            time += 1
        else:
            # Move in current direction
            if direction == "right":
                next_pos = pos + 1
                if next_pos >= m:
                    # Hit concrete wall on right
                    direction = "left"
                    time += 1
                elif floors[floor][next_pos] == '#':
                    # Hit concrete wall
                    direction = "left"
                    time += 1
                elif floors[floor][next_pos] == '+':
                    # Hit brick, break it and change direction
                    floors[floor][next_pos] = '.'
                    direction = "left"
                    time += 1

                    # Move to empty cell
                    pos = next_pos
                    time += 1

                next_pos = pos - 1
                if next_pos < 0:
                    # Hit concrete wall on left
```

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read floor descriptions
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(list(input().strip()))
    
    # Joe starts at floor n-1 (0-indexed), position 0, direction "right"
    floor = n - 1
    pos = 0
    direction = "right"
    time = 0
    
    # Track visited states to detect cycles
    visited = set()
    
    while floor > 0:
        # Create state key for cycle detection
        state = (floor, pos, direction)
        if state in visited:
            print("Never")
            return
            
        visited.add(state)
        
        # Check if we can fall down
        below_pos = pos
        below_floor = floor - 1
        
        if floors[below_floor][below_pos] == '.':
            # Fall down
            floor -= 1
            time += 1
        else:
            # Move in current direction
            if direction == "right":
                next_pos = pos + 1
                if next_pos >= m:
                    # Hit concrete wall on right
                    direction = "left"
                    time += 1
                elif floors[floor][next_pos] == '#':
                    # Hit concrete wall
                    direction = "left"
                    time += 1
                elif floors[floor][next_pos] == '+':
                    # Hit brick, break it and change direction
                    floors[floor][next_pos] = '.'
                    direction = "left"
                    time += 1
                else:
                    # Move to empty cell
                    pos = next_pos
                    time += 1
            else:  # direction == "left"
                next_pos = pos - 1
                if next_pos < 0:
                    # Hit concrete wall on left
                    direction = "right"
                    time += 1
                elif floors[floor][next_pos] == '#':
                    # Hit concrete wall
                    direction = "right"
                    time += 1
                elif floors[floor][next_pos] == '+':
                    # Hit brick, break it and change direction
                    floors[floor][next_pos] = '.'
                    direction = "right"
                    time += 1
                else:
                    # Move to empty cell
                    pos = next_pos
                    time += 1
    
    print(time)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Joe has been hurt on the Internet. Now he is storming around the house, destroying everything in his path.

Joe's house has n floors, each floor is a segment of m cells. Each cell either contains nothing (it is an empty cell), or has a brick or a concrete wall (always something one of three). It is believed that each floor is surrounded by a concrete wall on the left and on the right.

Now Joe is on the n-th floor and in the first cell, counting from left to right. At each moment of time, Joe has the direction of his gaze, to the right or to the left (always one direction of the two). Initially, Joe looks to the right.

Joe moves by a particular algorithm. Every second he makes one of the following actions:   If the cell directly under Joe is empty, then Joe falls down. That is, he moves to this cell, the gaze direction is preserved.  Otherwise consider the next cell in the current direction of the gaze.   If the cell is empty, then Joe moves into it, the gaze direction is preserved.  If this cell has bricks, then Joe breaks them with his forehead (the cell becomes empty), and changes the direction of his gaze to the opposite.  If this cell has a concrete wall, then Joe just changes the direction of his gaze to the opposite (concrete can withstand any number of forehead hits).  

Joe calms down as soon as he reaches any cell of the first floor.

The figure below shows an example Joe's movements around the house.

 [Image] 

Determine how many seconds Joe will need to calm down.


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers n and m (2 ≤ n ≤ 100, 1 ≤ m ≤ 10^4).

Next n lines contain the description of Joe's house. The i-th of these lines contains the description of the (n - i + 1)-th floor of the house — a line that consists of m characters: "." means an empty cell, "+" means bricks and "#" means a concrete wall.

It is guaranteed that the first cell of the n-th floor is empty.


-----Output-----

Print a single number — the number of seconds Joe needs to reach the first floor; or else, print word "Never" (without the quotes), if it can never happen.

Please, do not use the %lld specifier to read or write 64-bit integers in С++. It is preferred to use the cin, cout streams or the %I64d specifier.


-----Examples-----
Input
3 5
..+.#
#+..+
+.#+.

Output
14
Input
4 10
...+.##+.+
+#++..+++#
++.#++++..
.+##.++#.+

Output
42

Input
2 2
..

Output
Never

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. Joe starts on the n-th floor (top floor) at position 0 (first cell), looking right
2. Each second, Joe performs one of these actions:
   - If cell directly below is empty, he falls down (moves down, direction preserved)
   - Otherwise, he looks in current direction:
     - If next cell is empty, he moves there (direction preserved)
     - If next cell has bricks ("+"), he breaks it (becomes empty), changes direction
     - If next cell has concrete wall ("#"), he changes direction only
3. Joe stops when he reaches any cell on the first floor
4. We need to count seconds until he reaches first floor, or return "Never" if impossible

## Key Insights

1. This is a simulation problem with state tracking
2. State consists of: current floor, current position, current direction
3. We need to detect cycles to avoid infinite loops
4. If Joe never reaches floor 1, we return "Never"

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Parse input to get the house configuration
2. Simulate Joe's movements second by second
3. Track visited states (floor, position, direction) to detect cycles
4. If we reach floor 0 (first floor), return the time
5. If we detect a cycle without reaching floor 0, return "Never"

## Implementation

```python
def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read the floors - n-th floor is first in input
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # Joe starts at floor n-1 (0-indexed), position 0, direction = 0 (right)
    # We'll use a set to track visited states to detect cycles
    visited = set()
    
    floor = n - 1  # starting at top floor (n-1 in 0-indexed)
    pos = 0        # starting at first cell
    direction = 1  # 1 = right, -1 = left (initially right)
    time = 0
    
    # We'll simulate until Joe reaches floor 0 or we detect a cycle
    while floor > 0:  # while not on first floor
        # Create state tuple for cycle detection
        state = (floor, pos, direction)
        
        # If we've seen this state before, we're in a cycle
        if state in visited:
            print("Never")
            return
            
        visited.add(state)
        
        # Check if we can fall down
        if floor > 0 and floors[floor-1][pos] == '.':
            # Can fall down - move to the cell below
            floor -= 1
            time += 1
        else:
            # Can't fall down - look in current direction
            next_pos = pos + direction
            
            # Check if next position is valid (within bounds)
            if next_pos < 0 or next_pos >= m:
                # Out of bounds - this means we hit a wall
                # Change direction only
                direction *= -1
                time += 1
            else:
                cell = floors[floor][next_pos]
                if cell == '.':
                    # Empty cell - move there
                    pos = next_pos
                    time += 1
                elif cell == '+':
                    # Brick - break it, change direction
                    floors[floor] = floors[floor][:next_pos] + '.' + floors[floor][next_pos+1:]
                    direction *= -1
                    time += 1
                elif cell == '#':
                    # Concrete wall - change direction only
                    direction *= -1
                    time += 1
    
    # Joe reached first floor
    print(time)

solve()
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. Joe starts at the bottom floor (n-th floor) in the first cell, looking right
2. Each second, Joe performs one of these actions:
   - If cell directly below is empty, he falls down
   - Otherwise, he looks in current direction and:
     - If next cell is empty: moves there (direction unchanged)
     - If next cell has bricks: breaks bricks (cell becomes empty), changes direction
     - If next cell has concrete wall: changes direction only
3. Joe stops when he reaches any cell on the first floor
4. We need to find how many seconds this takes, or "Never" if it never happens

## Key Observations

1. Joe moves in a sequence of actions that might loop
2. We need to detect cycles to avoid infinite loops
3. State can be represented by: (current_floor, current_column, direction)
4. When Joe falls down, he moves to the cell below, maintaining direction
5. When he encounters bricks or walls, he changes direction

## Algorithm Approach

1. Use BFS or simulation with visited states to detect cycles
2. Each state: (floor, column, direction)
3. For each state, simulate one second of movement
4. If we reach floor 1, return the time
5. If we encounter a state we've seen before, return "Never"
6. Otherwise continue simulation

## Implementation Strategy

1. Parse input
2. Use a set to track visited states
3. Use a queue to store (floor, column, direction, time) for BFS
4. For each state, simulate the next move according to the rules
5. Handle falling down, moving to empty cells, breaking bricks, and wall encounters

```python
from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read floors - store in reverse order since input is top-down but we start from bottom
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # Joe starts at floor n-1 (0-indexed), column 0, looking right (1 for right, 0 for left)
    # We need to find when he reaches floor 0
    
    # State: (floor, column, direction) where direction: 0=left, 1=right
    visited = set()
    queue = deque()
    
    # Initial state
    start_floor = n - 1
    start_col = 0
    start_direction = 1  # right
    
    queue.append((start_floor, start_col, start_direction, 0))
    visited.add((start_floor, start_col, start_direction))
    
    while queue:
        floor, col, direction, time = queue.popleft()
        
        # Check if we reached first floor (floor 0)
        if floor == 0:
            return time
        
        # If we've seen this state before, we're in a cycle
        if (floor, col, direction) in visited:
            continue
            
        # Mark current state as visited
        visited.add((floor, col, direction))
        
        # Determine next action
        next_floor = floor
        next_col = col
        next_direction = direction
        
        # Action 1: If cell directly below is empty, Joe falls down
        below_col = col
        if floor < n - 1 and floors[floor + 1][below_col] == '.':
            # Fall down to next floor
            next_floor = floor + 1
            # The cell he falls to becomes occupied (was empty, now occupied by Joe)
            # Note: the original cell (current position) is still Joe here
            # But we need to track where Joe moves to
            # After falling, he's at (next_floor, col)
            # The direction remains the same
            next_direction = direction
        else:
            # Look in current direction
            # Calculate next cell in current direction
            if direction == 1:  # right
                next_col = col + 1
            else:  # left
                next_col = col - 1
            
            # Check if this next cell is valid and what it contains
            if next_col < 0 or next_col >= m:
                # This should not happen due to wall boundaries
                # But if next_col is out of bounds, it must be a wall
                # Since left/right of first/last cell are walls
                next_direction = 1 - direction
            else:
                cell_content = floors[floor][next_col]
                if cell_content == '.':
                    # Empty cell - move there
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = direction
                elif cell_content == '+':
                    # Brick - break it, change direction
                    # The cell becomes empty
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = 1 - direction
                elif cell_content == '#':
                    # Concrete wall - change direction only
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = 1 - direction
        
        # After all actions, we have next state
        # But we need to consider if Joe might fall down from current position
        # Actually, we need to reconsider the logic - the falling down check should be first
        
        # Let me rework this - the falling down happens first
        # Only if the cell below is empty, then fall down immediately
        
        # Actually, I think I made a mistake in the logic flow
        
        # Let me restart with correct logic:
        
        # Check if falling down is possible
        if floor < n - 1 and floors[floor + 1][col] == '.':
            # Fall down
            next_floor = floor + 1
            next_col = col
            next_direction = direction
        else:
            # Look in current direction
            if direction == 1:  # right
                next_col = col + 1
            else:  # left
                next_col = col - 1
            
            # Check if next_col is valid
            if next_col < 0 or next_col >= m:
                # We hit a wall - change direction
                next_direction = 1 - direction
            else:
                cell_content = floors[floor][next_col]
                if cell_content == '.':
                    # Empty cell - move there
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = direction
                elif cell_content == '+':
                    # Brick - break it, change direction
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = 1 - direction
                elif cell_content == '#':
                    # Concrete wall - change direction
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = 1 - direction
        
        # Now check if the next state is valid and not visited
        next_state = (next_floor, next_col, next_direction)
        if next_state not in visited:
            queue.append((next_floor, next_col, next_direction, time + 1))
    
    # If we exit the loop, it means we never reached floor 0
    return "Never"

print(solve())
```

```python
from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read floors - n lines, floors[0] is top, floors[n-1] is bottom
    # But Joe starts at bottom floor, so we need floors[n-1] to floors[0]
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # Joe starts at floor n-1 (bottom), column 0, direction = right (1)
    # We'll use BFS to simulate until he reaches floor 0
    
    # State: (floor, col, direction) where direction: 0=left, 1=right
    visited = set()
    queue = deque()
    
    queue.append((n-1, 0, 1))  # floor, col, direction (1=right, 0=left)
    visited.add((n-1, 0, 1))
    
    time = 0
    
    while queue:
        # Get current state
        floor, col, direction = queue.popleft()
        time += 1
        
        # Check if we reached first floor (floor 0)
        if floor == 0:
            return time
        
        # Check if we need to fall down
        if floor < n - 1 and floors[floor + 1][col] == '.':
            # Joe falls down to next floor (floor + 1)
            next_floor = floor + 1
            next_col = col
            next_direction = direction
        else:
            # Look in current direction
            if direction == 1:  # right
                next_col = col + 1
            else:  # left
                next_col = col - 1
            
            # Check if next_col is within bounds
            if next_col < 0 or next_col >= m:
                # Must be a wall - change direction
                next_direction = 1 - direction
            else:
                cell_content = floors[floor][next_col]
                if cell_content == '.':
                    # Empty cell - move there
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = direction
                elif cell_content == '+':
                    # Brick - break it, change direction
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = 1 - direction
                elif cell_content == '#':
                    # Concrete wall - change direction
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = 1 - direction
        
        # If we didn't reach floor 0, check if next state is new
        next_state = (next_floor, next_col, next_direction)
        if next_state not in visited:
            visited.add(next_state)
            queue.append(next_state)
    
    return "Never"
```

from collections import deque

def solve():
    n, m = map(int, input().split())
    
    # Read floors - n lines, floors[0] is top, floors[n-1] is bottom
    # But Joe starts at bottom floor, so we need floors[n-1] to floors[0]
    floors = []
    for _ in range(n):
        floors.append(input().strip())
    
    # Joe starts at floor n-1 (bottom), column 0, direction = right (1)
    # We'll use BFS to simulate until he reaches floor 0
    
    # State: (floor, col, direction) where direction: 0=left, 1=right
    visited = set()
    queue = deque()
    
    queue.append((n-1, 0, 1))  # floor, col, direction (1=right, 0=left)
    visited.add((n-1, 0, 1))
    
    time = 0
    
    while queue:
        # Get current state
        floor, col, direction = queue.popleft()
        time += 1
        
        # Check if we reached first floor (floor 0)
        if floor == 0:
            return time
        
        # Check if we need to fall down
        if floor < n - 1 and floors[floor + 1][col] == '.':
            # Joe falls down to next floor (floor + 1)
            next_floor = floor + 1
            next_col = col
            next_direction = direction
        else:
            # Look in current direction
            if direction == 1:  # right
                next_col = col + 1
            else:  # left
                next_col = col - 1
            
            # Check if next_col is within bounds
            if next_col < 0 or next_col >= m:
                # Must be a wall - change direction
                next_direction = 1 - direction
            else:
                cell_content = floors[floor][next_col]
                if cell_content == '.':
                    # Empty cell - move there
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = direction
                elif cell_content == '+':
                    # Brick - break it, change direction
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = 1 - direction
                elif cell_content == '#':
                    # Concrete wall - change direction
                    next_floor = floor
                    next_col = next_col
                    next_direction = 1 - direction
        
        # If we didn't reach floor 0, check if next state is new
        next_state = (next_floor, next_col, next_direction)
        if next_state not in visited:
            visited.add(next_state)
            queue.append(next_state)
    
    return "Never"

print(solve())

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

At the big break Nastya came to the school dining room. There are $n$ pupils in the school, numbered from $1$ to $n$. Unfortunately, Nastya came pretty late, so that all pupils had already stood in the queue, i.e. Nastya took the last place in the queue. Of course, it's a little bit sad for Nastya, but she is not going to despond because some pupils in the queue can agree to change places with some other pupils.

Formally, there are some pairs $u$, $v$ such that if the pupil with number $u$ stands directly in front of the pupil with number $v$, Nastya can ask them and they will change places. 

Nastya asks you to find the maximal number of places in queue she can move forward. 


-----Input-----

The first line contains two integers $n$ and $m$ ($1 \leq n \leq 3 \cdot 10^{5}$, $0 \leq m \leq 5 \cdot 10^{5}$) — the number of pupils in the queue and number of pairs of pupils such that the first one agrees to change places with the second one if the first is directly in front of the second.

The second line contains $n$ integers $p_1$, $p_2$, ..., $p_n$ — the initial arrangement of pupils in the queue, from the queue start to its end ($1 \leq p_i \leq n$, $p$ is a permutation of integers from $1$ to $n$). In other words, $p_i$ is the number of the pupil who stands on the $i$-th position in the queue.

The $i$-th of the following $m$ lines contains two integers $u_i$, $v_i$ ($1 \leq u_i, v_i \leq n, u_i \neq v_i$), denoting that the pupil with number $u_i$ agrees to change places with the pupil with number $v_i$ if $u_i$ is directly in front of $v_i$. It is guaranteed that if $i \neq j$, than $v_i \neq v_j$ or $u_i \neq u_j$. Note that it is possible that in some pairs both pupils agree to change places with each other.

Nastya is the last person in the queue, i.e. the pupil with number $p_n$.


-----Output-----

Print a single integer — the number of places in queue she can move forward.


-----Examples-----
Input
2 1
1 2
1 2

Output
1
Input
3 3
3 1 2
1 2
3 1
3 2

Output
2
Input
5 2
3 1 5 4 2
5 2
5 4

Output
1


-----Note-----

In the first example Nastya can just change places with the first pupil in the queue.

Optimal sequence of changes in the second example is   change places for pupils with numbers $1$ and $3$.  change places for pupils with numbers $3$ and $2$.  change places for pupils with numbers $1$ and $2$. 

The queue looks like $[3, 1, 2]$, then $[1, 3, 2]$, then $[1, 2, 3]$, and finally $[2, 1, 3]$ after these operations.

The input will be stdin and you should print your solution to stdout

Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

## Problem Understanding

```python
from collections import deque

def main():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read
    data = input().split()
    
    # Parse first line
    n = int(data[0])
    m = int(data[1])
    
    # Parse second line
    p = list(map(int, data[2:2+n]))
    
    # Parse next m lines
    valid_swaps = set()
    index = 2 + n
    for _ in range(m):
        u = int(data[index])
        v = int(data[index+1])
        index += 2
        valid_swaps.add((u, v))
    
    # BFS with distance
    if n == 1:
        print(0)
        return
        
    queue = deque()
    visited = set()
    arrangement = p[:]
    
    # Start from last position with distance 0
    queue.append((n-1, 0))
    visited.add(n-1)
    max_forward = 0
    
    while queue:
        pos, dist = queue.popleft()
        # Try to swap with the person in front (at pos-1)
        if pos > 0:
            front_pupil = arrangement[pos-1]
            current_pupil = arrangement[pos]
            if (current_pupil, front_pupil) in valid_swaps:
                # Swap them
                arrangement[pos-1], arrangement[pos] = arrangement[pos], arrangement[pos-1]
                new_pos = pos - 1
                if new_pos not in visited:
                    visited.add(new_pos)
                    new_dist = dist + 1
                    queue.append((new_pos, new_dist))
                    if new_dist > max_forward:
                        max_forward = new_dist
    
    print(max_forward)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```python
from collections import deque

def main():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read
    data = input().split()
    
    # Parse first line
    n = int(data[0])
    m = int(data[1])
    
    # Parse second line
    p = list(map(int, data[2:2+n]))
    
    # Parse next m lines
    valid_swaps = set()
    index = 2 + n
    for _ in range(m):
        u = int(data[index])
        v = int(data[index+1])
        index += 2
        valid_swaps.add((u, v))
        valid_swaps.add((v, u))  # both directions are valid if both agree (or by the problem's note)
    
    # BFS with distance
    if n == 1:
        print(0)
        return
        
    queue = deque()
    visited = set()
    arrangement = p[:]
    
    # Start from last position with distance 0
    queue.append((n-1, 0))
    visited.add(n-1)
    max_forward = 0
    
    while queue:
        pos, dist = queue.popleft()
        # Try to swap with the person in front (at pos-1)
        if pos > 0:
            front_pupil = arrangement[pos-1]
            current_pupil = arrangement[pos]
            if (current_pupil, front_pupil) in valid_swaps:
                # Swap them
                arrangement[pos-1], arrangement[pos] = arrangement[pos], arrangement[pos-1]
                new_pos = pos - 1
                if new_pos not in visited:
                    visited.add(new_pos)
                    new_dist = dist + 1
                    queue.append((new_pos, new_dist))
                    if new_dist > max_forward:
                        max_forward = new_dist
    
    print(max_forward)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```python
from collections import deque

def main():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read
    data = input().split()
    
    n = int(data[0])
    m = int(data[1])
    p = list(map(int, data[2:2+n]))
    
    valid_swaps = set()
    index = 2 + n
    for _ in range(m):
        u = int(data[index])
        v = int(data[index+1])
        index += 2
        valid_swaps.add((u, v))
        valid_swaps.add((v, u))
    
    if n == 1:
        print(0)
        return
        
    queue = deque()
    visited = set()
    arrangement = p[:]
    
    queue.append((n-1, 0))
    visited.add(n-1)
    max_forward = 0
    
    while queue:
        pos, dist = queue.popleft()
        if pos > 0:
            front_pupil = arrangement[pos-1]
            current_pupil = arrangement[pos]
            if (current_pupil, front_pupil) in valid_swaps:
                arrangement[pos-1], arrangement[pos] = arrangement[pos], arrangement[pos-1]
                new_pos = pos - 1
                if new_pos not in visited:
                    visited.add(new_pos)
                    new_dist = dist + 1
                    queue.append((new_pos, new_dist))
                    if new_dist > max_forward:
                        max_forward = new_dist
    
    print(max_forward)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
