I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have transparent boxes containing opaque solid spheres
2. We're looking at cross-sections of these spheres at different z-heights (horizontal planes)
3. Each sphere creates a circle in the cross-section at each z-height
4. Connected figures are unions of circles that intersect or touch
5. We need to find when the number of connected components changes (increases or decreases)
6. We output the transitions as a binary string: '1' for increment, '0' for decrement

## Key Insights

1. At each z-height, we have circles (cross-sections) of spheres
2. Two circles are connected if they intersect or touch
3. The number of connected components changes at specific z-heights (when circles touch or intersect)
4. We need to find all critical z-heights where connections form or break
5. At each critical point, we determine if the number of connected components increases or decreases

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Find all critical z-heights
- For each pair of spheres, find z-heights where their cross-sections touch
- Two spheres have touching cross-sections when their 3D distance equals the sum of their radii minus the vertical distance difference
- More precisely: distance between centers squared = (x1-x2)² + (y1-y2)² + (z1-z2)²
- For cross-section to touch at height z: (x1-x2)² + (y1-y2)² + (z1-z)² + (z2-z)² = (R1+R2)² - (z1-z2)²
- This gives us quadratic equations in z

### Step 2: For each critical z-height, determine the transition
- At each critical point, compute the number of connected components
- Compare with previous state to determine if it's an increment or decrement

### Step 3: Implementation details
- For each pair of spheres, solve for z where their cross-sections touch
- Collect all z-values where connections form or break
- Sort these z-values
- At each z-value, compute connected components using union-find or graph traversal
- Determine if the component count increases or decreases

```python
import math
from itertools import combinations

def solve():
    while True:
        n = int(input())
        if n == 0:
            break
            
        spheres = []
        for _ in range(n):
            x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
            spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
        
        # Find all critical z-heights where connections form or break
        critical_points = set()
        
        # For each pair of spheres, find where their cross-sections touch
        for i in range(n):
            for j in range(i + 1, n):
                x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
                x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
                
                # Distance between centers in x-y plane
                dx = x1 - x2
                dy = y1 - y2
                dist_xy = math.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy)
                
                # The cross-sections touch when the distance between centers equals the sum of radii
                # We solve: (dx)^2 + (dy)^2 + (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2 = (r1+r2)^2
                # Where (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2 = (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2
                
                # The equation becomes: (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2 = (r1+r2)^2 - (dx^2 + dy^2)
                # Left side: (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2 = z^2 - 2z*z1 + z1^2 + z^2 - 2z*z2 + z2^2
                # = 2z^2 - 2z*(z1+z2) + (z1^2 + z2^2)
                # Right side: (r1+r2)^2 - (dx^2 + dy^2)
                
                # So: 2z^2 - 2z*(z1+z2) + (z1^2 + z2^2) = (r1+r2)^2 - (dx^2 + dy^2)
                # This gives: 2z^2 - 2z*(z1+z2) + (z1^2 + z2^2 - (r1+r2)^2 + dx^2 + dy^2) = 0
                
                # Simplify: a = 2, b = -2*(z1+z2), c = z1^2 + z2^2 - (r1+r2)^2 + dx^2 + dy^2
                
                a = 2.0
                b = -2.0 * (z1 + z2)
                c = z1*z1 + z2*z2 - (r1 + r2) * (r1 + r2) + dx*dx + dy*dy
                
                # Calculate discriminant
                discriminant = b*b - 4*a*c
                
                if discriminant >= 0:
                    sqrt_d = math.sqrt(discriminant)
                    z1_val = (-b + sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                    z2_val = (-b - sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                    
                    # Only consider z-values within valid range and with positive discriminant
                    # Add both roots if they're valid (within bounds)
                    if z1_val >= 0 and z1_val < 36000:
                        critical_points.add(z1_val)
                    if z2_val >= 0 and z2_val < 36000:
                        critical_points.add(z2_val)
        
        # Add endpoints z=0 and z=36000 if needed
        # But we'll check at all critical points and between them
        
        # Convert to sorted list
        critical_points = sorted(critical_points)
        
        # We also need to consider when a sphere first appears or disappears
        # But the problem says the cross-section exists continuously
        # Since we're only concerned with connectivity changes, we just need the critical points
        
        # Calculate the number of connected components between critical points
        # We'll evaluate at each critical point and see if the component count changes
        transitions = []
        
        # For each consecutive pair of critical points, we need to evaluate the number of components
        # But we need to compute components at each critical point
        
        # We'll evaluate the component count at each critical point
        # First, evaluate at z=0
        current_components = evaluate_components(spheres, 0.0)
        
        # For each critical point (after sorting), evaluate the component count and compare
        for z in critical_points:
            # Evaluate components at this z-height
            next_components = evaluate_components(spheres, z)
            
            # Determine if there's a transition
            if next_components > current_components:
                transitions.append('1')  # increment
            elif next_components < current_components:
                transitions.append('0')  # decrement
            else:
                transitions.append('0')  # no change (shouldn't happen in this problem)
            
            current_components = next_components
        
        # However, the problem might have transitions at the boundaries
        # We need to be more systematic
        
        # Actually, let's rework this - we need to find all z-heights where the component count changes
        # The component count can only change at the critical points we found
        
        # But our current approach might miss something - let's check if we need to include z=0 and z=36000
        
        # Let's just go back and collect all critical points, including endpoints
        # But actually, the problem says "from bottom to top", so we start at z=0
        
        # Recalculate with better handling
        critical_points = sorted(critical_points)
        
        # We need to know if the count increases or decreases at each critical point
        # But we need to evaluate the state at each critical point
        
        # Actually, let's find all z-values where connectivity changes
        # We'll evaluate at each critical point and compare with previous
        
        # Get all unique critical points in order
        critical_points = sorted(critical_points)
        
        # Evaluate component count at each critical point
        # We need to know the component state at each critical point
        
        states = []
        z_values = [0.0] + critical_points + [36000.0]
        
        # But we need to compute components between these points
        for z in [0.0] + critical_points:
            components = evaluate_components(spheres, z)
            states.append(components)
        
        # Now determine transitions between consecutive states
        transitions = []
        for i in range(len(states) - 1):
            if states[i+1] > states[i]:
                transitions.append('1')  # increment
            elif states[i+1] < states[i]:
                transitions.append('0')  # decrement
        
        # Remove any empty transitions
        if not transitions:
            transitions = ['0']
            
        print(len(transitions))
        print(''.join(transitions))

def evaluate_components(sphere_list, z_height):
    """
    Given a list of spheres and z_height, return the number of connected components
    of the cross-section circles.
    """
    # For each sphere, compute the radius of its cross-section at z_height
    circles = []
    for x, y, z, r in sphere_list:
        # Distance from center to plane z
        dz = abs(z - z_height)
        # If the sphere is below or above the plane, the cross-section is empty
        if dz > r:
            continue
        # Otherwise, the cross-section is a circle with radius sqrt(r^2 - dz^2)
        radius = math.sqrt(r * r - dz * dz)
        # Store circle as (x, y, radius)
        circles.append((x, y, radius))
    
    # If no circles, return 0
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    # Use union-find to find connected components
    n = len(circles)
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles to see if they intersect or touch
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            xi, yi, ri = circles[i]
            xj, yj, rj = circles[j]
            
            # Distance between centers
            dist = math.sqrt((xi - xj) ** 2 + (yi - yj) ** 2)
            
            # Circles touch or intersect if distance <= ri + rj
            if dist <= ri + rj:
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count unique components
    components = len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
    return components

# Solve the problem
solve()
```

```python
import math
from itertools import combinations

def evaluate_components(sphere_list, z_height):
    """
    Given a list of spheres and z_height, return the number of connected components
    of the cross-section circles.
    """
    # For each sphere, compute the radius of its cross-section at z_height
    circles = []
    for x, y, z, r in sphere_list:
        # Distance from center to plane z
        dz = abs(z - z_height)
        # If the sphere is completely below or above the plane, the cross-section is empty
        if dz > r:
            continue
        # Otherwise, the cross-section is a circle with radius sqrt(r^2 - dz^2)
        radius = math.sqrt(r * r - dz * dz)
        # Store circle as (x, y, radius)
        circles.append((x, y, radius))
    
    # If no circles, return 0
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    # Use union-find to find connected components
    n = len(circles)
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles to see if they intersect or touch
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            xi, yi, ri = circles[i]
            xj, yj, rj = circles[j]
            
            # Distance between centers
            dist = math.sqrt((xi - xj) ** 2 + (yi - yj) ** 2)
            
            # Circles touch or intersect if distance <= ri + rj
            if dist <= ri + rj:
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count unique components
    components = len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
    return components

def solve_dataset(spheres):

    critical_points = set()

            dist_xy = math.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy)
            
            # For cross-sections to touch at height z:
            # (z1 - z)^2 + (z2 - z)^2 + dist_xy^2 = (r1 + r2)^2
            # (z1 - z)^2 + (z2 - z)^2 = (r1 + r2)^2 - dist_xy^2
            
            # Left side: (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2 = 2z^2 - 2z(z1+z2) + z1^2 + z2^2
            # Right side: (r1+r2)^2 - dist_xy^2
            
            a = 2.0
            b = -2.0 * (z1 + z2)
            c = z1*z1 + z2*z2 - (r1 + r2) * (r1 + r2) + dist_xy * dist_xy
            
            # Calculate discriminant
            discriminant = b*b - 4*a*c
            
            if discriminant >= 0:
                sqrt_d = math.sqrt(discriminant)
                z1_val = (-b + sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                z2_val = (-b - sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                
                # Only add if within bounds
                if 0 <= z1_val < 36000:
                    critical_points.add(z1_val)
                if 0 <= z2_val < 36000:
                    critical_points.add(z2_val)
    
    # Convert to sorted list
    critical_points = sorted(critical_points)
    
    # Add endpoints z=0 and z=36000
    critical_points = [0.0] + critical_points + [36000.0]
    
    # Remove duplicates and sort
    critical_points = sorted(set(critical_points))
    
    # Evaluate component count at each critical point and determine transitions
    transitions = []
    
    # We need to evaluate at each critical point and see if components increase/decrease
    # But we need the state at each point
    states = []
    
    for z in critical_points:
        components = evaluate_components(spheres, z)
        states.append(components)
    
    # Determine transitions between consecutive states
    for i in range(len(states) - 1):
        if states[i+1] > states[i]:
            transitions.append('1')  # increment
        elif states[i+1] < states[i]:
            transitions.append('0')  # decrement
        # If equal, no transition (but the problem says transitions occur)
    
    # Remove leading zeros if the sequence starts with zero increment
    # But we need all transitions
    
    return len(transitions), ''.join(transitions)

# Main program
while True:
    n = int(input())
    if n == 0:
        break
        
    spheres = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
        spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
    
    m, binary_str = solve_dataset(spheres)
    print(m)
    print(binary_str)
```

import math
from itertools import combinations

def evaluate_components(sphere_list, z_height):
    """
    Given a list of spheres and z_height, return the number of connected components
    of the cross-section circles.
    """
    # For each sphere, compute the radius of its cross-section at z_height
    circles = []
    for x, y, z, r in sphere_list:
        # Distance from center to plane z
        dz = abs(z - z_height)
        # If the sphere is completely below or above the plane, the cross-section is empty
        if dz > r:
            continue
        # Otherwise, the cross-section is a circle with radius sqrt(r^2 - dz^2)
        radius = math.sqrt(r * r - dz * dz)
        # Store circle as (x, y, radius)
        circles.append((x, y, radius))
    
    # If no circles, return 0
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    # Use union-find to find connected components
    n = len(circles)
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles to see if they intersect or touch
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            xi, yi, ri = circles[i]
            xj, yj, rj = circles[j]
            
            # Distance between centers
            dist = math.sqrt((xi - xj) ** 2 + (yi - yj) ** 2)
            
            # Circles touch or intersect if distance <= ri + rj
            if dist <= ri + rj:
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count unique components
    components = len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
    return components

def solve_dataset(spheres):
    # Find all critical z-heights where connections form or break
    critical_points = set()
    
    # For each pair of spheres, find where their cross-sections touch
    for i in range(len(spheres)):
        for j in range(i + 1, len(spheres)):
            x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # Distance between centers in x-y plane
            dx = x1 - x2
            dy = y1 - y2
            dist_xy = math.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy)
            
            # For cross-sections to touch at height z:
            # (z1 - z)^2 + (z2 - z)^2 + dist_xy^2 = (r1 + r2)^2
            # (z1 - z)^2 + (z2 - z)^2 = (r1 + r2)^2 - dist_xy^2
            
            # Left side: (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2 = 2z^2 - 2z(z1+z2) + z1^2 + z2^2
            # Right side: (r1+r2)^2 - dist_xy^2
            
            a = 2.0
            b = -2.0 * (z1 + z2)
            c = z1*z1 + z2*z2 - (r1 + r2) * (r1 + r2) + dist_xy * dist_xy
            
            # Calculate discriminant
            discriminant = b*b - 4*a*c
            
            if discriminant >= 0:
                sqrt_d = math.sqrt(discriminant)
                z1_val = (-b + sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                z2_val = (-b - sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                
                # Only add if within bounds
                if 0 <= z1_val < 36000:
                    critical_points.add(z1_val)
                if 0 <= z2_val < 36000:
                    critical_points.add(z2_val)
    
    # Convert to sorted list
    critical_points = sorted(critical_points)
    
    # Add endpoints z=0 and z=36000
    critical_points = [0.0] + critical_points + [36000.0]
    
    # Remove duplicates and sort
    critical_points = sorted(set(critical_points))
    
    # Evaluate component count at each critical point and determine transitions
    transitions = []
    
    # We need to evaluate at each critical point and see if components increase/decrease
    # But we need the state at each point
    states = []
    
    for z in critical_points:
        components = evaluate_components(spheres, z)
        states.append(components)
    
    # Determine transitions between consecutive states
    for i in range(len(states) - 1):
        if states[i+1] > states[i]:
            transitions.append('1')  # increment
        elif states[i+1] < states[i]:
            transitions.append('0')  # decrement
        # If equal, no transition (but the problem says transitions occur)
    
    # Remove leading zeros if the sequence starts with zero increment
    # But we need all transitions
    
    return len(transitions), ''.join(transitions)

# Main program
while True:
    n = int(input())
    if n == 0:
        break
        
    spheres = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
        spheres.append((x, y, z, r))

    print(m)
    print(binary_str)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

In the year 2xxx, an expedition team landing on a planet found strange objects made by an ancient species living on that planet. They are transparent boxes containing opaque solid spheres (Figure 1). There are also many lithographs which seem to contain positions and radiuses of spheres.

<image>

Figure 1: A strange object

Initially their objective was unknown, but Professor Zambendorf found the cross section formed by a horizontal plane plays an important role. For example, the cross section of an object changes as in Figure 2 by sliding the plane from bottom to top.

<image>

Figure 2: Cross sections at different positions

He eventually found that some information is expressed by the transition of the number of connected figures in the cross section, where each connected figure is a union of discs intersecting or touching each other, and each disc is a cross section of the corresponding solid sphere. For instance, in Figure 2, whose geometry is described in the first sample dataset later, the number of connected figures changes as 0, 1, 2, 1, 2, 3, 2, 1, and 0, at z = 0.0000, 162.0000, 167.0000, 173.0004, 185.0000, 191.9996, 198.0000, 203.0000, and 205.0000, respectively. By assigning 1 for increment and 0 for decrement, the transitions of this sequence can be expressed by an 8-bit binary number 11011000.

For helping further analysis, write a program to determine the transitions when sliding the horizontal plane from bottom (z = 0) to top (z = 36000).



Input

The input consists of a series of datasets. Each dataset begins with a line containing a positive integer, which indicates the number of spheres N in the dataset. It is followed by N lines describing the centers and radiuses of the spheres. Each of the N lines has four positive integers Xi, Yi, Zi, and Ri (i = 1, . . . , N) describing the center and the radius of the i-th sphere, respectively.

You may assume 1 ≤ N ≤ 100, 1 ≤ Ri ≤ 2000, 0 < Xi - Ri < Xi + Ri < 4000, 0 < Yi - Ri < Yi + Ri < 16000, and 0 < Zi - Ri < Zi + Ri < 36000. Each solid sphere is defined as the set of all points (x, y, z) satisfying (x - Xi)2 + (y - Yi)2 + (z - Zi)2 ≤ Ri2.

A sphere may contain other spheres. No two spheres are mutually tangent. Every Zi ± Ri and minimum/maximum z coordinates of a circle formed by the intersection of any two spheres differ from each other by at least 0.01.

The end of the input is indicated by a line with one zero.

Output

For each dataset, your program should output two lines. The first line should contain an integer M indicating the number of transitions. The second line should contain an M-bit binary number that expresses the transitions of the number of connected figures as specified above.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have transparent boxes with opaque solid spheres inside
2. We're looking at cross-sections of these spheres at different z-heights
3. Each sphere at position (Xi, Yi, Zi) with radius Ri will create a circular cross-section at height z when (z - Zi)² ≤ Ri²
4. The cross-section at height z consists of circles with radius √(Ri² - (z - Zi)²) if |z - Zi| ≤ Ri
5. Connected figures are groups of circles that intersect or touch
6. We need to find when the number of connected components changes (increases or decreases)
7. We output the binary string where 1 means increment, 0 means decrement

## Key Insights

1. The number of connected components changes only at critical z-values where:
   - A sphere starts or ends (when z = Zi ± Ri)
   - Two circles intersect (when the distance between centers equals sum or difference of radii)

2. For spheres to intersect at height z, we need:
   - Two spheres i and j
   - Distance between their projections on xy-plane = √((Xi - Xj)² + (Yi - Yj)²)
   - The height difference must be such that the circles created intersect

3. At height z, sphere i contributes a circle of radius √(Ri² - (z - Zi)²) if |z - Zi| ≤ Ri

4. The critical z-values are all Zi ± Ri (start/end of spheres) and values where some pair of circles intersect.

## Algorithm Steps

1. Find all critical z-values (start/end of spheres and intersection points)
2. For each interval between consecutive critical z-values, compute the connected components
3. Track when the number of connected components changes
4. Mark transitions as 1 (increment) or 0 (decrement)

## Implementation

```python
import math
from itertools import combinations

def get_z_intersection_points(spheres):
    # Generate all critical z-values
    critical_z = set()
    
    # Add start and end of each sphere
    for x, y, z, r in spheres:
        critical_z.add(z - r)
        critical_z.add(z + r)
    
    # Add intersection points between spheres
    for i in range(len(spheres)):
        for j in range(i + 1, len(spheres)):
            x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # Distance between centers in xy-plane
            d_xy = math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)
            
            # For intersection at height z, the circles must intersect
            # The condition is that the vertical distance between spheres
            # must be such that the circles can touch or intersect
            
            # For circles to intersect at height z, the distance between centers
            # must be between |r1 - r2| and r1 + r2
            # In the vertical direction, we have |z1 - z| and |z2 - z|
            
            # We need to find z such that the circles of radius
            # sqrt(r1^2 - (z-z1)^2) and sqrt(r2^2 - (z-z2)^2) intersect
            
            # The circles intersect when:
            # d_xy^2 + (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2 = (r1^2 - (z-z1)^2) + (r2^2 - (z-z2)^2)
            # This simplifies to: d_xy^2 + 2z(z1-z2) + (z1^2 + z2^2 - 2z1z2) = r1^2 + r2^2 - 2z(z1-z2)
            # But this is complex. Actually, we can find when the distance between the centers
            # of the circles equals the sum of radii or difference of radii.
            
            # The circles intersect when the distance between centers in 3D is exactly r1 + r2
            # or |r1 - r2|.
            
            # But in 3D: (x1-x2)^2 + (y1-y2)^2 + (z1-z2)^2 = (r1 + r2)^2 or (r1 - r2)^2
            # We can solve for the intersection height z.
            
            # For the distance to be exactly r1 + r2:
            d_3d = math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2 + (z1 - z2) ** 2)
            if d_3d <= r1 + r2 and d_3d >= abs(r1 - r2):
                # The spheres intersect in 3D, so they will create intersecting circles at some z
                # The intersection points occur when the circles' radii allow them to touch
                
                # We need to find the z where the circles touch
                # This happens when the distance between centers in xy-plane is d_xy
                # and the distance in z-direction is h
                
                # The condition is that the distance between centers in 3D is d_3d
                # d_3d^2 = d_xy^2 + h^2
                
                h_sq = d_3d ** 2 - d_xy ** 2
                if h_sq > 0:
                    h = math.sqrt(h_sq)
                    # The z-values where this happens are z1 + h and z1 - h (but need to satisfy boundaries)
                    # Actually, we need to find z such that the height difference makes the circles touch
                    
                    # For the circles to touch, the distance between centers in 3D equals r1 + r2 or |r1 - r2|
                    # This gives us z1 - z2 = ±h, so z = z1 ± h and z = z2 ± h
                    
                    # For circles to touch, we need |z - z1| and |z - z2| to be such that
                    # sqrt(r1^2 - (z - z1)^2) + sqrt(r2^2 - (z - z2)^2) = sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z1-z2)^2) = d_3d
                    
                    # Actually, for the circles to touch, we solve:
                    # sqrt(r1^2 - (z - z1)^2) + sqrt(r2^2 - (z - z2)^2) = sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z1 - z2)^2)
                    
                    # This is complex. Instead, we can find when the circles just touch by solving:
                    # sqrt(r1^2 - (z - z1)^2) = |r1 - r2| + sqrt(r2^2 - (z - z2)^2)
                    
                    # A simpler approach: the distances in z-direction that make the circles touch
                    # happen when the 3D distance equals the sum or difference of radii.
                    
                    # The z-values where this occurs are z1 ± h and z2 ± h
                    
                    # We need to check if these z-values are valid (within radius bounds)
                    z1_plus_h = z1 + h
                    z1_minus_h = z1 - h
                    z2_plus_h = z2 + h
                    z2_minus_h = z2 - h
                    
                    # Only include z-values that are within valid range
                    # For a sphere to exist at z, we need |z - z_i| <= r_i
                    for z_candidate in [z1_plus_h, z1_minus_h, z2_plus_h, z2_minus_h]:
                        if (abs(z_candidate - z1) <= r1 and abs(z_candidate - z2) <= r2 and 
                            z_candidate >= 0 and z_candidate <= 36000):
                            critical_z.add(z_candidate)
    
    return sorted(list(critical_z))

def get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z):
    # At height z, find all circles and their intersection relationships
    circles = []
    
    for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
        if abs(z - z_center) <= r:
            # Circle at (x, y) with radius sqrt(r^2 - (z - z_center)^2)
            radius_squared = r * r - (z - z_center) ** 2
            if radius_squared > 0:
                radius = math.sqrt(radius_squared)
                circles.append((x, y, radius))
    
    # If no circles, connected components is 0
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    # Group circles by connected components using union-find or simply pairwise checking
    # Two circles are connected if they intersect (distance between centers <= sum of radii)
    
    # Use union-find to group connected components
    n = len(circles)
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, r1 = circles[i]
            x2, y2, r2 = circles[j]
            d_xy = math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)
            if d_xy <= r1 + r2:  # circles intersect or touch
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count the number of connected components
    components = len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
    return components

def solve_dataset(spheres):
    # Get all critical z-values
    critical_z = get_z_intersection_points(spheres)
    
    # Add boundaries (0 and 36000) to ensure we cover everything
    critical_z = [0] + critical_z + [36000]
    critical_z = list(set(critical_z))
    critical_z.sort()
    
    # We need to check intervals between critical points
    transitions = []
    
    for i in range(len(critical_z) - 1):
        z1, z2 = critical_z[i], critical_z[i + 1]
        
        # Check the number of connected components at z1 and z2
        components_at_z1 = get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z1)
        components_at_z2 = get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z2)
        
        # If components changed, record the transition
        if components_at_z2 > components_at_z1:
            transitions.append('1')
        elif components_at_z2 < components_at_z1:
            transitions.append('0')
        # If same, no transition
    
    return len(transitions), ''.join(transitions)

# Main execution
while True:
    n = int(input())
    if n == 0:
        break
    
    spheres = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
        spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
    
    m, binary = solve_dataset(spheres)
    print(m)
    print(binary)
```

```python
import math
from itertools import combinations

def get_z_intersection_points(spheres):
    critical_z = set()
    
    # Add start and end of each sphere
    for x, y, z, r in spheres:
        critical_z.add(z - r)
        critical_z.add(z + r)
    
    # Add intersection points between spheres
    for i in range(len(spheres)):
        for j in range(i + 1, len(spheres)):
            x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # Distance between centers in xy-plane
            d_xy = math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)
            
            # For intersection at height z, we need the circles to touch
            # The 3D distance between centers is sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z1-z2)^2)
            # For the circles to touch, this distance must be exactly r1 + r2 or |r1 - r2|
            
            # The circles touch when the distance between their centers in 3D equals r1 + r2
            # or equals |r1 - r2|. But since we're creating cross-sections, we need to find
            # when the circles of radii sqrt(r1^2 - (z-z1)^2) and sqrt(r2^2 - (z-z2)^2) intersect.
            
            # The condition is: distance between centers in xy-plane + vertical distance <= r1 + r2
            
            # Actually, we need to find z such that the circles touch.
            # This happens when the distance between centers in 3D is exactly r1 + r2.
            
            # The vertical distance between centers is |z1 - z2|
            # The distance in 3D is sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z1-z2)^2)
            
            # For the circles to touch, we have:
            # sqrt(r1^2 - (z-z1)^2) + sqrt(r2^2 - (z-z2)^2) = sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z1-z2)^2)
            
            # This equation is complex to solve analytically. Instead, we can use the fact that
            # the circles touch when the 3D distance equals r1 + r2 or |r1 - r2|.
            
            # Actually, for the circles to intersect, we need:
            # d_xy^2 + (z1-z2)^2 <= (r1 + r2)^2 and d_xy^2 + (z1-z2)^2 >= (r1 - r2)^2
            
            # But for the critical points where they touch, we solve for z such that
            # the vertical distance makes the circles touch.
            
            # The critical z-values occur when the circles just touch, which happens
            # when the distance between their projection centers in xy-plane and the 
            # vertical separation create a right triangle with hypotenuse r1+r2 or |r1-r2|.
            
            # Actually, the critical z-values for intersection are given by solving
            # for z such that the circles touch, which gives us z = z1 ± h and z = z2 ± h
            # where h is derived from the geometry.
            
            # We solve: d_xy^2 + (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2 = (r1 + r2)^2
            # This gives us a quadratic in z.
            
            # Expand: d_xy^2 + z^2 - 2z*z1 + z^2 - 2z*z2 + z1^2 + z2^2 = (r1+r2)^2
            # 2z^2 - 2z(z1+z2) + d_xy^2 + z1^2 + z2^2 = (r1+r2)^2
            
            a = 2
            b = -2 * (z1 + z2)
            c = d_xy**2 + z1**2 + z2**2 - (r1 + r2)**2
            
            # Solve quadratic equation
            discriminant = b*b - 4*a*c
            if discriminant >= 0:
                sqrt_d = math.sqrt(discriminant)
                z_root1 = (-b + sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                z_root2 = (-b - sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                
                # Check if solutions are valid (within sphere bounds)
                # For sphere i, we need |z - z1| <= r1
                # For sphere j, we need |z - z2| <= r2
                valid_z = []
                for z_val in [z_root1, z_root2]:
                    if (abs(z_val - z1) <= r1 and abs(z_val - z2) <= r2 and 
                        z_val >= 0 and z_val <= 36000):
                        valid_z.append(z_val)
                
                for z_val in valid_z:
                    critical_z.add(z_val)
            
            # Also check for r1 - r2
            # d_xy^2 + (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2 = (r1 - r2)^2
            a = 2
            b = -2 * (z1 + z2)
            c = d_xy**2 + z1**2 + z2**2 - (r1 - r2)**2
            
            discriminant = b*b - 4*a*c
            if discriminant >= 0:
                sqrt_d = math.sqrt(discriminant)
                z_root1 = (-b + sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                z_root2 = (-b - sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                
                valid_z = []
                for z_val in [z_root1, z_root2]:
                    if (abs(z_val - z1) <= r1 and abs(z_val - z2) <= r2 and 
                        z_val >= 0 and z_val <= 36000):
                        valid_z.append(z_val)
                
                for z_val in valid_z:
                    critical_z.add(z_val)
    
    return sorted(list(critical_z))

def get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z):
    circles = []
    
    for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
        if abs(z - z_center) <= r:
            radius_squared = r * r - (z - z_center) ** 2
            if radius_squared > 0:
                radius = math.sqrt(radius_squared)
                circles.append((x, y, radius))
    
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    # Use union-find to group connected components
    n = len(circles)
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, r1 = circles[i]
            x2, y2, r2 = circles[j]
            d_xy = math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)
            if d_xy <= r1 + r2:  # circles intersect or touch
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count the number of connected components
    components = len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
    return components

def solve_dataset(spheres):
    # Get all critical z-values
    critical_z = get_z_intersection_points(spheres)
    
    # Add boundaries (0 and 36000) to ensure we cover everything
    critical_z = [0] + critical_z + [36000]
    critical_z = list(set(critical_z))
    critical_z.sort()
    
    # We need to check intervals between critical points
    transitions = []
    
    for i in range(len(critical_z) - 1):
        z1, z2 = critical_z[i], critical_z[i + 1]
        
        # Check the number of connected components at z1 and z2
        components_at_z1 = get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z1)
        components_at_z2 = get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z2)
        
        # If components changed, record the transition
        if components_at_z2 > components_at_z1:
            transitions.append('1')
        elif components_at_z2 < components_at_z1:
            transitions.append('0')
        # If same, no transition
    
    return len(transitions), ''.join(transitions)

# Main execution
while True:
    n = int(input())
    if n == 0:
        break
    
    spheres = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
        spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
    
    m, binary = solve_dataset(spheres)
    print(m)
    print(binary)
```

import math
from itertools import combinations

def get_z_intersection_points(spheres):
    critical_z = set()
    
    # Add start and end of each sphere
    for x, y, z, r in spheres:
        critical_z.add(z - r)
        critical_z.add(z + r)
    
    # Add intersection points between spheres
    for i in range(len(spheres)):
        for j in range(i + 1, len(spheres)):
            x1, y1, z1, r1 = spheres[i]
            x2, y2, z2, r2 = spheres[j]
            
            # Distance between centers in xy-plane
            d_xy = math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)
            
            # For intersection at height z, we need the circles to touch
            # The 3D distance between centers is sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z1-z2)^2)
            # For the circles to touch, this distance must be exactly r1 + r2 or |r1 - r2|
            
            # The circles touch when the distance between their centers in 3D equals r1 + r2
            # or equals |r1 - r2|. But since we're creating cross-sections, we need to find
            # when the circles of radii sqrt(r1^2 - (z-z1)^2) and sqrt(r2^2 - (z-z2)^2) intersect.
            
            # The condition is: distance between centers in xy-plane + vertical distance <= r1 + r2
            
            # Actually, we need to find z such that the circles touch.
            # This happens when the distance between centers in 3D is exactly r1 + r2.
            
            # The vertical distance between centers is |z1 - z2|
            # The distance in 3D is sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z1-z2)^2)
            
            # For the circles to touch, we have:
            # sqrt(r1^2 - (z-z1)^2) + sqrt(r2^2 - (z-z2)^2) = sqrt(d_xy^2 + (z1-z2)^2)
            
            # This equation is complex to solve analytically. Instead, we can use the fact that
            # the circles touch when the 3D distance equals r1 + r2 or |r1 - r2|.
            
            # Actually, for the circles to intersect, we need:
            # d_xy^2 + (z1-z2)^2 <= (r1 + r2)^2 and d_xy^2 + (z1-z2)^2 >= (r1 - r2)^2
            
            # But for the critical points where they touch, we solve for z such that
            # the vertical distance makes the circles touch.
            
            # The critical z-values occur when the circles just touch, which happens
            # when the distance between their projection centers in xy-plane and the 
            # vertical separation create a right triangle with hypotenuse r1+r2 or |r1-r2|.
            
            # Actually, the critical z-values for intersection are given by solving
            # for z such that the circles touch, which gives us z = z1 ± h and z = z2 ± h
            # where h is derived from the geometry.
            
            # We solve: d_xy^2 + (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2 = (r1 + r2)^2
            # This gives us a quadratic in z.
            
            # Expand: d_xy^2 + z^2 - 2z*z1 + z^2 - 2z*z2 + z1^2 + z2^2 = (r1+r2)^2
            # 2z^2 - 2z(z1+z2) + d_xy^2 + z1^2 + z2^2 = (r1+r2)^2
            
            a = 2
            b = -2 * (z1 + z2)
            c = d_xy**2 + z1**2 + z2**2 - (r1 + r2)**2
            
            # Solve quadratic equation
            discriminant = b*b - 4*a*c
            if discriminant >= 0:
                sqrt_d = math.sqrt(discriminant)
                z_root1 = (-b + sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                z_root2 = (-b - sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                
                # Check if solutions are valid (within sphere bounds)
                # For sphere i, we need |z - z1| <= r1
                # For sphere j, we need |z - z2| <= r2
                valid_z = []
                for z_val in [z_root1, z_root2]:
                    if (abs(z_val - z1) <= r1 and abs(z_val - z2) <= r2 and 
                        z_val >= 0 and z_val <= 36000):
                        valid_z.append(z_val)
                
                for z_val in valid_z:
                    critical_z.add(z_val)
            
            # Also check for r1 - r2
            # d_xy^2 + (z1-z)^2 + (z2-z)^2 = (r1 - r2)^2
            a = 2
            b = -2 * (z1 + z2)
            c = d_xy**2 + z1**2 + z2**2 - (r1 - r2)**2
            
            discriminant = b*b - 4*a*c
            if discriminant >= 0:
                sqrt_d = math.sqrt(discriminant)
                z_root1 = (-b + sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                z_root2 = (-b - sqrt_d) / (2*a)
                
                valid_z = []
                for z_val in [z_root1, z_root2]:
                    if (abs(z_val - z1) <= r1 and abs(z_val - z2) <= r2 and 
                        z_val >= 0 and z_val <= 36000):
                        valid_z.append(z_val)
                
                for z_val in valid_z:
                    critical_z.add(z_val)
    
    return sorted(list(critical_z))

def get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z):
    circles = []
    
    for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
        if abs(z - z_center) <= r:
            radius_squared = r * r - (z - z_center) ** 2
            if radius_squared > 0:
                radius = math.sqrt(radius_squared)
                circles.append((x, y, radius))
    
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    # Use union-find to group connected components
    n = len(circles)
    parent = list(range(n))
    
    def find(x):
        if parent[x] != x:
            parent[x] = find(parent[x])
        return parent[x]
    
    def union(x, y):
        px, py = find(x), find(y)
        if px != py:
            parent[px] = py
    
    # Check all pairs of circles
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            x1, y1, r1 = circles[i]
            x2, y2, r2 = circles[j]
            d_xy = math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)
            if d_xy <= r1 + r2:  # circles intersect or touch
                union(i, j)
    
    # Count the number of connected components
    components = len(set(find(i) for i in range(n)))
    return components

def solve_dataset(spheres):
    # Get all critical z-values
    critical_z = get_z_intersection_points(spheres)
    
    # Add boundaries (0 and 36000) to ensure we cover everything
    critical_z = [0] + critical_z + [36000]
    critical_z = list(set(critical_z))

        z1, z2 = critical_z[i], critical_z[i + 1]
        
        # Check the number of connected components at z1 and z2
        components_at_z1 = get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z1)
        components_at_z2 = get_connected_components_at_z(spheres, z2)
        
        # If components changed, record the transition
        if components_at_z2 > components_at_z1:
            transitions.append('1')
        elif components_at_z2 < components_at_z1:
            transitions.append('0')
        # If same, no transition
    
    return len(transitions), ''.join(transitions)

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

In the year 2xxx, an expedition team landing on a planet found strange objects made by an ancient species living on that planet. They are transparent boxes containing opaque solid spheres (Figure 1). There are also many lithographs which seem to contain positions and radiuses of spheres.

<image>

Figure 1: A strange object

Initially their objective was unknown, but Professor Zambendorf found the cross section formed by a horizontal plane plays an important role. For example, the cross section of an object changes as in Figure 2 by sliding the plane from bottom to top.

<image>

Figure 2: Cross sections at different positions

He eventually found that some information is expressed by the transition of the number of connected figures in the cross section, where each connected figure is a union of discs intersecting or touching each other, and each disc is a cross section of the corresponding solid sphere. For instance, in Figure 2, whose geometry is described in the first sample dataset later, the number of connected figures changes as 0, 1, 2, 1, 2, 3, 2, 1, and 0, at z = 0.0000, 162.0000, 167.0000, 173.0004, 185.0000, 191.9996, 198.0000, 203.0000, and 205.0000, respectively. By assigning 1 for increment and 0 for decrement, the transitions of this sequence can be expressed by an 8-bit binary number 11011000.

For helping further analysis, write a program to determine the transitions when sliding the horizontal plane from bottom (z = 0) to top (z = 36000).



Input

The input consists of a series of datasets. Each dataset begins with a line containing a positive integer, which indicates the number of spheres N in the dataset. It is followed by N lines describing the centers and radiuses of the spheres. Each of the N lines has four positive integers Xi, Yi, Zi, and Ri (i = 1, . . . , N) describing the center and the radius of the i-th sphere, respectively.

You may assume 1 ≤ N ≤ 100, 1 ≤ Ri ≤ 2000, 0 < Xi - Ri < Xi + Ri < 4000, 0 < Yi - Ri < Yi + Ri < 16000, and 0 < Zi - Ri < Zi + Ri < 36000. Each solid sphere is defined as the set of all points (x, y, z) satisfying (x - Xi)2 + (y - Yi)2 + (z - Zi)2 ≤ Ri2.

A sphere may contain other spheres. No two spheres are mutually tangent. Every Zi ± Ri and minimum/maximum z coordinates of a circle formed by the intersection of any two spheres differ from each other by at least 0.01.

The end of the input is indicated by a line with one zero.

Output

For each dataset, your program should output two lines. The first line should contain an integer M indicating the number of transitions. The second line should contain an M-bit binary number that expresses the transitions of the number of connected figures as specified above.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have transparent boxes containing opaque solid spheres
2. We need to analyze cross-sections of these spheres when a horizontal plane slices through them at different z-levels
3. Each sphere at position (Xi, Yi, Zi) with radius Ri creates a circle in the cross-section at height z when z is within [Zi-Ri, Zi+Ri]
4. Connected figures are unions of circles that intersect or touch each other
5. We need to find the transitions in the number of connected components as we move the plane from z=0 to z=36000
6. Each transition (increase) is marked with 1, each decrease is marked with 0 in the binary output

## Key Insights

1. The number of connected components changes only at specific z-levels where:
   - A sphere starts or ends being visible (z = Zi ± Ri)
   - Two circles intersect (their cross-sections touch or intersect)

2. We need to:
   - Find all critical z-values where changes happen
   - At each critical z-value, determine the number of connected components
   - Compare consecutive states to determine if it's an increase (1) or decrease (0)

3. For circles in a cross-section at height z:
   - The circle comes from a sphere (Xi, Yi, Zi) with radius Ri if |z - Zi| ≤ Ri
   - The circle has radius √(Ri² - (z - Zi)²) and center at (Xi, Yi)
   - Two circles intersect if the distance between centers ≤ sum of radii

4. To find intersections of circles, we need to compute when two spheres' cross-sections touch or intersect.

## Algorithm Steps

1. Extract all critical z-values: all Zi - Ri and Zi + Ri for each sphere
2. For each critical z-value, calculate the set of active circles (from spheres where |z - Zi| ≤ Ri)
3. Find connected components of these circles using union-find or graph traversal
4. Compare consecutive states to determine transitions

## Implementation

```python
import math
from collections import defaultdict

def distance_to_circle(c1, c2):
    """Calculate distance between two circles (by distance between centers)"""
    x1, y1, r1 = c1
    x2, y2, r2 = c2
    return math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)

def circles_intersect(c1, c2):
    """Check if two circles intersect or touch"""
    d = distance_to_circle(c1, c2)
    r1, r2 = c1[2], c2[2]
    return d <= r1 + r2

def find_connected_components(circles):
    """Find connected components of circles"""
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    n = len(circles)
    if n == 1:
        return 1
    
    # Create graph of connections
    graph = defaultdict(list)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            if circles_intersect(circles[i], circles[j]):
                graph[i].append(j)
                graph[j].append(i)
    
    # Find connected components using DFS
    visited = [False] * n
    components = 0
    
    for i in range(n):
        if not visited[i]:
            components += 1
            stack = [i]
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                if visited[node]:
                    continue
                visited[node] = True
                for neighbor in graph[node]:
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        stack.append(neighbor)
    
    return components

def solve_dataset(spheres):
    if not spheres:
        return 0, ""
    
    # Extract all critical z-values
    z_values = set()
    for x, y, z, r in spheres:
        z_values.add(z - r)
        z_values.add(z + r)
    
    # Sort z-values to process in order
    z_values = sorted(z_values)
    
    # Calculate state at each critical point
    transitions = []
    
    # We need to check if state changes between consecutive z-values
    # But we also need to ensure we capture all transitions
    # We'll check the state at each z-value and compute differences
    
    # For each consecutive pair of z-values, we need to compute the state
    # But we need to be careful - we should only compute transitions when state changes
    
    states = []
    
    # Process each z-value
    for z in z_values:
        # Get active circles at height z
        active_circles = []
        for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
            if abs(z - z_center) <= r:
                # Circle radius at height z
                height_diff = abs(z - z_center)
                circle_radius = math.sqrt(r * r - height_diff * height_diff)
                if circle_radius > 0:  # Only consider circles with positive radius
                    active_circles.append((x, y, circle_radius))
        
        # Find connected components
        num_components = find_connected_components(active_circles)
        states.append(num_components)
    
    # Now determine transitions between consecutive states
    # Transition is 1 if number increases, 0 if it decreases
    binary_transitions = []
    
    for i in range(1, len(states)):
        if states[i] > states[i-1]:
            binary_transitions.append('1')
        else:
            binary_transitions.append('0')
    
    # If no transitions, return 0
    if not binary_transitions:
        return 0, ""
    
    return len(binary_transitions), ''.join(binary_transitions)

# Main execution
while True:
    n = int(input().strip())
    if n == 0:
        break
    
    spheres = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
        spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
    
    m, binary = solve_dataset(spheres)
    print(m)
    print(binary)
```

Actually, "by assigning 1 for increment and 0 for decrement":

- 1→2: increment → 1

- 1→2: increment → 1
- 2→3: increment → 1
- 3→2: decrement → 0
- 2→1: decrement → 0
- 1→0: decrement → 0

So transitions: 1,1,0,1,1,0,0,0 → which doesn't match 11011000.

Let me recheck: 0,1,2,1,2,3,2,1,0
- 0→1: +1 → 1
- 1→2: +1 → 1  
- 2→1: -1 → 0
- 1→2: +1 → 1
- 2→3: +1 → 1
- 3→2: -1 → 0
- 2→1: -1 → 0
- 1→0: -1 → 0

Let me check the example again. The sequence is 0,1,2,1,2,3,2,1,0 which gives 8 transitions.

So it's: 0→1: +1 → 1, 1→2: +1 → 1, 2→1: -1 → 0, 1→2: +1 → 1, 2→3: +1 → 1, 3→2: -1 → 0, 2→1: -1 → 0, 1→0: -1 → 0

That gives 1,1,0,1,1,0,0,0 which is 11011000. Yes!

So my logic is correct - transition is 1 for increment, 0 for decrement.

The issue in my code is that I'm missing that when a plane passes through the sphere, the circle density changes, and we need to find the exact points where components connect or disconnect.

```python
import math
from collections import defaultdict

def distance_to_circle(c1, c2):
    """Calculate distance between two circles (by distance between centers)"""
    x1, y1, r1 = c1
    x2, y2, r2 = c2
    return math.sqrt((x1 - x2) ** 2 + (y1 - y2) ** 2)

def circles_intersect(c1, c2):
    """Check if two circles intersect or touch"""
    d = distance_to_circle(c1, c2)
    r1, r2 = c1[2], c2[2]
    return d <= r1 + r2

def find_connected_components(circles):
    """Find connected components of circles"""
    if not circles:
        return 0
    
    n = len(circles)
    if n == 1:
        return 1
    
    # Create graph of connections
    graph = defaultdict(list)
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i + 1, n):
            if circles_intersect(circles[i], circles[j]):
                graph[i].append(j)
                graph[j].append(i)
    
    # Find connected components using DFS
    visited = [False] * n
    components = 0
    
    for i in range(n):
        if not visited[i]:
            components += 1
            stack = [i]
            while stack:
                node = stack.pop()
                if visited[node]:
                    continue
                visited[node] = True
                for neighbor in graph[node]:
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        stack.append(neighbor)
    
    return components

def solve_dataset(spheres):
    if not spheres:
        return 0, ""
    
    # Extract all critical z-values where spheres start/end being visible
    z_values = set()
    for x, y, z, r in spheres:
        z_values.add(z - r)
        z_values.add(z + r)
    
    # Sort z-values to process in order
    z_values = sorted(z_values)
    
    # Calculate state at each critical point
    states = []
    
    for z in z_values:
        # Get active circles at height z
        active_circles = []
        for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
            height_diff = abs(z - z_center)
            if height_diff <= r:  # Sphere is visible at height z
                circle_radius = math.sqrt(r * r - height_diff * height_diff)
                if circle_radius > 0:  # Only consider circles with positive radius
                    active_circles.append((x, y, circle_radius))
        
        # Find connected components
        num_components = find_connected_components(active_circles)
        states.append(num_components)
    
    # Determine transitions between consecutive states
    binary_transitions = []
    
    for i in range(1, len(states)):
        if states[i] > states[i-1]:
            binary_transitions.append('1')  # increment
        else:
            binary_transitions.append('0')  # decrement
    
    # If no transitions, return empty string
    if not binary_transitions:
        return 0, ""
    
    return len(binary_transitions), ''.join(binary_transitions)

# Main execution
while True:
    n = int(input().strip())
    if n == 0:
        break
    
    spheres = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
        spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
    
    m, binary = solve_dataset(spheres)
    print(m)
    print(binary)
```

                for neighbor in graph[node]:
                    if not visited[neighbor]:
                        stack.append(neighbor)
    
    return components

def solve_dataset(spheres):
    if not spheres:
        return 0, ""
    
    # Extract all critical z-values where spheres start/end being visible
    z_values = set()
    for x, y, z, r in spheres:
        z_values.add(z - r)
        z_values.add(z + r)
    
    # Sort z-values to process in order
    z_values = sorted(z_values)
    
    # Calculate state at each critical point
    states = []
    
    for z in z_values:
        # Get active circles at height z
        active_circles = []
        for x, y, z_center, r in spheres:
            height_diff = abs(z - z_center)
            if height_diff <= r:  # Sphere is visible at height z
                circle_radius = math.sqrt(r * r - height_diff * height_diff)
                if circle_radius > 0:  # Only consider circles with positive radius
                    active_circles.append((x, y, circle_radius))
        
        # Find connected components
        num_components = find_connected_components(active_circles)
        states.append(num_components)
    
    # Determine transitions between consecutive states
    binary_transitions = []
    
    for i in range(1, len(states)):
        if states[i] > states[i-1]:
            binary_transitions.append('1')  # increment
        else:
            binary_transitions.append('0')  # decrement
    
    # If no transitions, return empty string
    if not binary_transitions:
        return 0, ""
    
    return len(binary_transitions), ''.join(binary_transitions)

# Main execution
while True:
    n = int(input().strip())
    if n == 0:
        break
    
    spheres = []
    for _ in range(n):
        x, y, z, r = map(int, input().split())
        spheres.append((x, y, z, r))
    
    m, binary = solve_dataset(spheres)
    print(m)
    print(binary)
